# Evidence register

Sources: [RESEARCH.md](RESEARCH.md), the plan of record; [Santos et al.](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0296864) section 7 for R1.

The results register: one entry per experiment, reproduction or paid generation run, holding its configuration, its result and what the
result was read against. The criteria live in [RESEARCH.md](RESEARCH.md); an entry links the
section that holds them rather than restating them. An interactive session of
[`bko.app`](../src/bko/app/README.md) is not a run of record: it reads no criterion, and its run
folder is its record.

## How to read an entry

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Status | `not yet run`, `running` or `complete` |
| Question | What the experiment answers |
| Configuration | Everything needed to rerun it: dataset and split with the dataset snapshot's identity (download date and checksum; for Synthea its `run.json`: jar checksum, seeds, snapshot date, population size and CSV checksums), the generator's model id, sampling parameters and prompt, baselines and controls, metrics and library versions, seeds, the commit, and how the readings [RESEARCH.md](RESEARCH.md#4-success-criteria) leaves open were fixed |
| Runs | One dated row per run: run id, date, commit, where its logs and artifacts live (its run folder under `runs/`, or the `scratchpad/` paths a row names; [DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#runs)), and, for runs that call a paid API, its API spend, the model the API reported, its token counts and where its request/response log lives. A rerun adds a row; a superseded or failed run keeps its row with the reason |
| Result | What was measured, with units, and the command that produced it. Empty until the status is `complete` |
| Read against | The RESEARCH.md section whose criteria judge the result, and the verdict: met, not met, inconclusive (its intervals decide neither way), or a null result reported as such |

## R1. MONDO and DO fusion reproduction

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | Does `bko.bkb`, given MONDO and DO fragments built from the pinned releases, rebuild the fusion example of Santos et al. (section 7 of [the paper](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0296864)), and where does it differ? |
| Configuration | Command: `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.ontology reproduce`. Code: `bko.ontology.santos` holds the paper's terms, PTAs, anchor, passes and misprints; `bko.ontology.fragments` the encoding (ADR `research/bko-fragment-encoding`). Environment: Python 3.14, pronto 2.7.3 (`uv.lock`). Releases: MONDO 2026-10-06 and DO 2026-09-30 as `bko.datasets.sources` pins them, where the paper used MONDO 2023-09-12 and DO 2023-10-21. Bridge: MONDO's `skos:exactMatch` annotations, where the paper queried the EMBL-EBI OxO service. Fusion: weight 1 per fragment, `fuse`'s default (each source prior over every fragment's weight, the paper's section 6.1). Instantiation: from the paper's anchor (MONDO's lesion of sciatic nerve, DO's sciatic neuropathy), each pass adds every rule whose antecedents hold, as the paper's algorithm states. Misprints read as the releases give them: Table 1's DOID:114466 is DOID:11446; the fifth MONDO PTA is printed as a second TM4 with antecedent Mononeuritis, and MONDO gives neuritis (MONDO:0002122) as the subclass of inflammatory disease; section 7.1 narrates two bridge rules in its second pass whose antecedents are the anchor |
| Runs | `r1-1` · 2026-10-17 · commit `a8ddddb` on `feat/mondo-do-fusion` (kept in the PR's history) · superseded by `r1-2`: review fixes changed `bko.ontology.santos` after it · no API spend<br>`r1-2` · 2026-10-17 · commit `05e93b3` on `feat/mondo-do-fusion` (kept in the PR's history) · superseded by `r1-4` (rerun at `run/rerun-1`) · stdout and stderr in `scratchpad/reproduce.csv` and `scratchpad/reproduce.err` on the runner's machine (gitignored); exit 0 · a second machine, Linux x86_64 under WSL2, with Python 3.14.4 and pronto 2.7.3 (`uv.lock`); releases fetched on that machine: MONDO `releases/2026-10-06`, sha256 `2631e8e165d41def1a46749adb1ce0a336c8aba7f6bc0978729592cee8fed60b`, and DO `releases/2026-09-30`, sha256 `51e717b6b9f5d391f2793f9e16faf9c8629a661ab8f21c3a024458129f182ad2` · stdout and stderr in `scratchpad/pr1-reproduce.csv` and `scratchpad/pr1-reproduce.err` on the runner's machine (gitignored), sha256 `7a059dce04ee11cb64df42254171a56c0d807adf1273c4eded27c690f187631e` and `1761217fd505e97a58c72f3bcd63bb9922b3e977b1451df45ee78b64a08a0e04` · 40 rows: 34 `match`, 4 `erratum`, 2 `reported`, none `differs`; exit 0 (the script echoes `FAIL r1-4` on a nonzero exit, and `scratchpad/rerun/wave0.out` holds no such line) · pronto 2.7.3 (`parser pronto 2.7.3`); releases from its `snapshot present` and `release` lines: MONDO `releases/2026-10-06`, sha256 `2631e8e165d41def1a46749adb1ce0a336c8aba7f6bc0978729592cee8fed60b`, and DO `releases/2026-09-30/doid.obo`, sha256 `51e717b6b9f5d391f2793f9e16faf9c8629a661ab8f21c3a024458129f182ad2` · sha256: `reproduction.csv` `7a059dce04ee11cb64df42254171a56c0d807adf1273c4eded27c690f187631e`, `reproduction.log` `5b0616fcf308704cc47e0e05309f874dad188ada857671f516e172e01886b89f` · 40 rows: 34 `match`, 4 `erratum`, 2 `reported`, none `differs`; its log ends `reproduced 40 findings: 0 differ`; `cmp runs/r1-2/reproduction.csv runs/r1-4/reproduction.csv` equal (exit 0), and the CSV's sha256 is `r1-3`'s stdout's · no API spend |
| Result | `r1-4` (`bko.ontology reproduce`, the command `make reproduce` runs; its Runs row), `runs/r1-4/reproduction.csv`: no row `differs` of 40. The 11 terms and 17 PTAs are found; DOID:114466 and the fifth MONDO PTA are `erratum` rows. Instantiation from the anchor adds TM1, TD1, TB2 and TB3, then TM2, TD2, TB1 and TB4, where section 7.1 narrates TM1 and TD1, then TM2, TD2 and TB1 to TB4 (`erratum`: TB2 and TB3 take the anchor as their antecedents); the same 8 after two passes, and all 17 at the fixpoint. Inferences a and b: 0.3333333333333333 each against the paper's 0.33; under pybkb's normalization a is 0.5, so the paper's figure follows from its algorithm's every-fragment denominator. The chain DOID:11446 -> DOID:12528 -> DOID:9473 -> DOID:1802 -> MONDO:0002121 -> MONDO:0002122 -> MONDO:0021166 exists in the fused BKB and in neither fragment nor the whole MONDO release; its product is 0.001371742112482853, one source prior of 1/3 per S-node, where the paper gives no figure. Beyond the command: the fused BKB is not mutually exclusive, since MONDO's two S-nodes on mononeuropathy share their source (`tests/test_ontology_santos.py`); and the chain's first two DO steps are axioms the pinned MONDO excludes: `relationship: excluded_subClassOf MONDO:0001543 {source="DOID:11446", source="https://orcid.org/0000-0001-5208-3432"} ! lesion of sciatic nerve` and `relationship: excluded_subClassOf MONDO:0004797 {source="DOID:12528", source="https://orcid.org/0000-0001-5208-3432"} ! mononeuritis of lower limb` |
| Read against | [RESEARCH.md: Detect](RESEARCH.md#detect); verdict: met |

## E1. UCI Heart Disease augmentation

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | Does BKO-guided augmentation on UCI Heart Disease meet the success criteria against the baselines and controls? |
| Configuration | `bko.evaluate.experiment.E1` holds the values and ADR [`research/e1-evaluation-protocol`](adr/research/e1-evaluation-protocol.md) the protocol and its reasons. Data: the UCI archive `bko.datasets.sources` names, split by `bko.datasets.split` at each of `E1`'s holdout seeds. Arms: no augmentation, class weighting, SMOTE, ADASYN, CTGAN, TVAE and three BKO arms (`bko`, the candidate, and `bko-population` and `bko-frequency`, which no criterion reads and whose measures are reported beside it); every arm is asked to fill every underrepresented cell up to the threshold of [RESEARCH.md](RESEARCH.md#2-intended-users-and-evaluation-setting) from the holdout's training rows; every row-adding arm, the four baselines and the three BKO arms, also fills a replica of the training split's cells ([RESEARCH.md section 5](RESEARCH.md#5-evaluation-contract)); a refused fill is retried with a derived seed, up to `E1`'s attempts. BKO arms (`bko.evaluate.guided`): each reads only the holdout's training rows, finds their gaps under its definition (`bko` per-subgroup, `bko-population` population-level, `bko-frequency` frequency-aware; ADR [`research/bko-formal-gap-definition`](adr/research/bko-formal-gap-definition.md)), guides each requested cell by them, asks the Messages API for each cell's shortfall round by round, validates every reply with `bko.validate`, drops each accepted candidate `bko.baselines.records.check` would refuse (by `bko.baselines.records.drops`: outside the training split's range, then in a cell the request does not ask for, then past its cell's quota), counts a candidate toward the cell its own values name, and returns the rest without clipping, rounding or repairing a value. Each BKO arm fills the replica request the same way. On the default split population-level grounding lists no gap ([E2](#e2-synthea-gap-detection-ablation)), so `bko-population` guides each cell by what its records show. Fusion: an arm's rows join the holdout's training rows (`bko.baselines.records.training_set`) before the classifier fits; gap closure after fusion is read by [C1](#c1-gap-closure-after-fusion), not here. Budget per attempt: `E1.budget` times the request's rows, counted in records asked; an attempt whose remaining budget cannot cover the rows still short is refused as a plain `BaselineError`. Spend ceiling: a run stops, unretried, once its call log's spend reaches `E1.spend_ceiling` USD; a call that gets no response also stops it. Spend and duration estimates: the `e1-4` entry of Runs holds the estimate stated before that run, and the `e1-5` entry the spend estimate stated before the judged run. Generator: `bko.generate.messages.SETTINGS`, `Settings("claude-haiku-5-5", "medium", 16000, 20, Level.SUMMARY)`, for all three arms, with the training-split bounds in the prompt: the level [G3](#g3-guidance-level) chose and the effort [G4](#g4-effort-at-summary) chose, as [G1](#g1-record-generation-through-the-messages-api) records them; no sampling parameter is sent and the API takes no seed; the prompt is `bko.generate.prompt` at the run's commit, and each call's request is in the call log. Classifier: `bko.evaluate.classifier`; the criteria read its logistic regression, and a histogram gradient boosting model fit on the same augmented rows on one OpenMP thread (threadpoolctl as `uv.lock` pins it) is scored beside it as `recall-hgb`, `precision-hgb` and `auc-pr-hgb` rows, its primary and secondary contrasts logged by `compare` as `contrast model=hgb` lines with no verdict; replica utility reads the regression only; ADR [`research/e1-evaluation-protocol`](adr/research/e1-evaluation-protocol.md) holds its settings and why no criterion reads it. Test split: on each holdout every arm fills before any is scored; a test record equal in every column to a row some arm added or replicated leaves the test split every arm's utility is scored on, and each arm's count of such rows is reported (`test-copies`); the held-out ratio and the test rows' distance to closest record use the whole split. How the criteria of [RESEARCH.md](RESEARCH.md#4-success-criteria) are read: primary, the candidate beats every comparator, however slightly, in both recall and AUC-PR over the whole test split, each paired difference's corrected interval lying above zero (`E1`'s margin is zero); secondary, the same over the flagged subgroups, the age-sex subgroups that hold an underrepresented cell; privacy, the 5th percentile of the added rows' distance to the training rows reaches `E1`'s share of the test rows' 5th percentile, its interval lying above it; completeness comes from the fusion stage ([C1](#c1-gap-closure-after-fusion)), not from this harness; validity is `bko`'s `passed` summed over `generated` summed across every `bko.validate` call it makes, in its gap and replica fills alike, on every holdout and in every refused attempt, a candidate dropped afterwards included, met when that rate is at least `E1.validity` and not met below it, read as a point with no interval, and refused when a fill generated nothing; each fill's counts and rate are reported apart, with no verdict; `bko-population`'s and `bko-frequency`'s rates and every BKO arm's dropped candidates are reported beside it. An interval criterion is met when every interval it reads lies above its threshold, not met when one lies below it at the family-wise level (each of its k intervals read at confidence 1 - (1 - c)/k, c being `E1`'s confidence), and inconclusive otherwise; a `compare` that refuses the judged run's scores, as it does an empty value a criterion reads, is E1's result. Reproduction: the baselines reproduce from seeds on one machine; a BKO arm's rows come from the paid run's call log, and `score --replay` answers every call from it and rewrites the scores byte for byte with no API call (ADR [`research/call-log-replay`](adr/research/call-log-replay.md)). Judged result: E1's latest configuration, as ADR [`research/e1-evaluation-protocol`](adr/research/e1-evaluation-protocol.md) (The latest configuration is judged) states; every run keeps its Runs row here, a failed one with its reason; `e1-5` is the judged run, `e1-4`'s configuration rerun at tag `run/rerun-1`; `e1-4` ran the same configuration at its commit `48098d0`, and this file at commit `d2790bf` holds its Result and Read against; `e1-3` ran `E1` at level `ontology` without the training-split bounds in the prompt, and this file at commit `48098d0` holds its Result and Read against; `e1-1` ran `E1` as it stood at its commit `a12d11c` (the BKO arms filled no replica, and `SETTINGS.model` was `claude-opus-5-5`), and this file at commit `e4a5507` holds its Result and Read against |

| Read against | [RESEARCH.md: success criteria](RESEARCH.md#4-success-criteria); verdicts: primary inconclusive, secondary inconclusive, privacy met, validity met, completeness not read here ([C1](#c1-gap-closure-after-fusion)). Inconclusive reads, under E1's Configuration, as no interval lying wholly above zero at confidence 0.95 and none lying below zero at the family-wise level: all 12 primary intervals and all 12 secondary intervals contain zero, so the run neither shows nor rules out a benefit, and `bko` is not shown better or worse than any comparator. With primary inconclusive, no benefit of knowledge guidance is shown on this frame, and this is reported as such, as RESEARCH section 4 commits. The printed `[low, high]` intervals are each read at confidence 0.95; only `refuting_high`, the upper bound at the family-wise level, decides not met, and its minimum over all 24 contrasts is +0.0125 (primary AUC-PR against smote). The intervals are wide: the printed upper bounds of the primary recall intervals run from +0.0287 to +0.0438 and of the flagged-subgroup recall intervals from +0.0303 to +0.0566; at the refuting level they run from +0.0458 to +0.0620 and from +0.0447 to +0.1015, so recall benefits of those sizes are not excluded. The primary AUC-PR upper bounds, +0.0078 to +0.0230 printed and +0.0125 to +0.0318 refuting, rule out a primary AUC-PR gain larger than about 0.03. Privacy met reads as its interval lying above zero: `bko`'s added rows sit farther from the training rows, at the 5th percentile, than `E1.privacy_margin` times the test rows do. Validity met reads as 25824/25832 at or above `E1.validity` (19/20). Replica utility and fidelity carry no verdict: no criterion of [RESEARCH.md section 4](RESEARCH.md#4-success-criteria) reads them, and [RESEARCH.md section 5](RESEARCH.md#5-evaluation-contract) reports fidelity with no pass threshold. `e1-5` is judged at level `summary` with the training-split bounds in the prompt, at effort `medium`; `e1-4`, the same configuration's earlier run (this file at commit `d2790bf`), and `e1-3`, at level `ontology` without the bounds and also at effort `medium` (the `settings` of each call log's `run` line; this file at commit `48098d0`), read the same four verdicts. `e1-5` and `e1-4` share `E1`'s configuration: their call logs' `run` lines hold the same `settings`, and both logs send one and the same system prompt; the code moved from `48098d0` to `2e80bd8` between them, and the API takes no seed, so a difference between them is the generator's run-to-run variation together with any effect of that code change. No paired comparison of `e1-5` against `e1-4` was made; one is feasible, the runs sharing their holdouts. Beside that, as point values with no interval, `bko` at `e1-4` then `e1-5`: whole-split recall 0.8107 then 0.8092, whole-split AUC-PR 0.9107 then 0.9101, flagged-subgroup AUC-PR 0.8476 then 0.8469, replica recall 0.6884 then 0.6836, replica quality 0.8194 then 0.8193, and range drops 0 of 25825 then 0 of 25832 (from each run's `mean`, `replica` and `validity` lines of its `criteria.log`). Replica quality 0.8193 is above `tvae`'s 0.8039, the same in both runs, and below `smote`'s 0.8869, `adasyn`'s 0.9218 and `ctgan`'s 0.8209. The primary recall intervals are narrower at `e1-5` against five of the six comparators (against none 0.0758 wide, `e1-4`'s 0.0868) and wider against `ctgan` (0.0785, `e1-4`'s 0.0722), and the comparators' means agree between the runs to within 0.0001: their scores differ only on holdouts 11 and 36, where one test record left the test split in one run and not the other. `e1-5`'s generator is Haiku 5.5, as `e1-3`'s and `e1-4`'s were, and `e1-1`'s was Opus 5.5 with no BKO replica, so a difference from `e1-1` is not attributable to either change alone. Second model: reported, no verdict (ADR `research/e1-evaluation-protocol`) [RESEARCH section 6](RESEARCH.md#limits-of-uci-heart-disease-as-the-evaluation-frame) says why this frame cannot read a smaller effect and which frame the project tests next. |

## E2. Synthea gap-detection ablation

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | Does BKO gap detection find the cases deliberately ablated from a Synthea population, and how do BKO-detected gaps compare with count-based ones? |
| Configuration | Commands: `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.gaps synthea`, and `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.gaps uci` for the UCI reading. Code: `bko.gaps.experiment` holds the values (`E2_RUN`, `E2_ABLATIONS`) and the reading; `bko.gaps.definitions` the three gap definitions of ADR [`research/bko-formal-gap-definition`](adr/research/bko-formal-gap-definition.md) and the frequency-aware rule of ADR [`research/frequency-aware-gap-score`](adr/research/frequency-aware-gap-score.md) with its constants; ADR [`research/dataset-fragment-encoding`](adr/research/dataset-fragment-encoding.md) the dataset fragments; ADR [`research/synthea-ablation-design`](adr/research/synthea-ablation-design.md) the ablation. Data: the population `E2_RUN` names (`bko.datasets.synthea_run.DEFAULT_RUN`), the patients alive on its snapshot date. Releases: MONDO as `bko.datasets.sources` pins it; no Synthea code maps to an HPO concept, so the Synthea BKO holds no HPO fragment. Scope: the classes the code mappings name and their MONDO `is_a` ancestors. Code mappings: `bko.ontology.mapping.CODE_MAPPINGS` maps seven Synthea disorder codes, ischemic heart disease, essential hypertension, type 2 diabetes, hyperlipidemia, myocardial infarction, ST-elevation myocardial infarction and stroke, each to the one live MONDO concept carrying its `skos:exactMatch` (ADR [`research/ontology-mapping-design`](adr/research/ontology-mapping-design.md)), a scope of 32 classes and 37 statements (the `scope` line of `e2-10`'s stderr); ST-elevation myocardial infarction maps by that rule beside myocardial infarction; acute non-ST-elevation myocardial infarction (SNOMED CT 401314000) is excluded (`UNMAPPED_CODES`), since no live MONDO concept carries its `skos:exactMatch`; `e2-0` to `e2-8` ran with ischemic heart disease alone (MONDO:0024644, 9 statements). Fusion: weight 1 per fragment, `fuse`'s default normalization; the gap lists do not depend on the weights. Ablations: `cell`, `DEFAULT_ABLATION`'s band and sex, and `condition`, every band and both sexes, each removing every patient with `ISCHEMIC_HEART_DISEASE` it selects, and the same two at `REMOVED_FRACTIONS`, named `cell-25`, `cell-50`, `cell-75`, `condition-25`, `condition-50` and `condition-75`, which remove that share of the selected patients, the first of a draw seeded by the spec's seed (`bko.datasets.ablation`), whole patients each. Sensitivity and specificity: per detector, sensitivity is the share of the eligible truth gaps recovered, and specificity the share of the items outside the truth, every (cell, statement) pair of the grid for a definition and every age-sex-label cell for the count-based detector, that are not new outside it; both read `n/a` when their denominator is empty (`bko.gaps.experiment.detection`, the `detection` lines of stderr). Cells: every detector reads the ablation's age bands, `SYNTHEA_AGE_BAND_EDGES`; the definitions read the 12 age-sex cells, which carry no label because the label is the ablated condition, and the count-based detector the 24 age-sex-label cells with the threshold of [RESEARCH.md](RESEARCH.md#2-intended-users-and-evaluation-setting). Definitions: per-subgroup grounds each cell against its own patients; population-level grounds every patient together, its gaps holding in all 12 cells; frequency-aware flags a cell's concept against every patient outside the cell. Reading, over (cell, statement) pairs for each definition and over cells for the count-based detector: the truth is the pairs whose statement the removed patients of the cell ground, whether or not the cell's other patients ground it too, and the cells whose count fell; a truth gap present before the removal is not eligible; an eligible gap present after it is recovered; a gap after the removal that lies outside the truth is outside it, and new outside it when it was absent before; a gap present before and absent after vanished. The reading and both ablations were fixed after the planning measurement `e2-0`; the per-cell reading, the three definitions and the frequency-aware rule were fixed before the planning measurement `e2-3`, which computed them as their records state them; the Result reads the seven-code scope from `e2-10`, and its one-code figures from `e2-4` (whole removals), `e2-7` (fraction ablations) and `e2-8` (first flags, from `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.gaps first-flag`, which removes each whole ablation's cases one at a time in its seed's draw order; `first_flag` counts the ablation's removed cases, so for `condition` it runs over all 261). Limits: in the one-code figures (`e2-4`, `e2-7`, `e2-8`) each definition's gaps in a cell are all 9 statements or none and every pair count is a multiple of 9; seven codes map in `e2-10`, and the `cell` ablation is still one cell's decision; the `condition` ablation removes ischemic heart disease patients whole, so the other mapped conditions they hold leave with them, and its truth holds statements the remaining patients still ground, such as the `is_a` ancestors myocardial ischemia shares with myocardial infarction (myocardial disorder, heart disorder), which no grounding definition can recover: per-subgroup's 61 of 261 and the `cell` ablation's 3 of 37 in `e2-10` read that shortfall as the richer scope's consequence, not a defect; ST-elevation myocardial infarction brings acute myocardial infarction and acute disease into scope, with 4 statements no other mapped concept reaches (ST-elevation myocardial infarction to acute myocardial infarction, acute myocardial infarction to myocardial infarction and to acute disease, acute disease to human disease), and myocardial infarction reaches no statement it does not (the `is_a` closure of each mapped concept in the pinned MONDO release, read with `bko.ontology.obo.parse_obo`); removal is complete and of whole patients, so for both grounding definitions every truth pair is eligible, and every statement reads from one class, so a per-subgroup gap new after a removal lies in the truth: per-subgroup's new outside and vanished counts, its recovery of every eligible pair with one code, and the count-based detector's new outside count of 0 follow from the definitions and check the implementation; outside counts every gap after the removal outside the truth, including absences the removal did not cause, such as the cells that held no patient with the condition, so it is context, not an error rate; with one mapped concept (`e2-4`, `e2-7`) a removal of its holders only lowers every other cell's reference share, so frequency-aware's new outside count of 0 there follows from the definitions; with seven (`e2-10`) the removed patients also hold other mapped concepts, so its new outside count of 0 there is measured, as are its vanished gaps and its recovery of the `cell` truth throughout; the partial ablations measure sensitivity and specificity; below a fraction of 1 a grounding definition sees a cell only where the draw removes every case of it, since one remaining record of the cell, or of the population, grounds a statement; the fractions of one spec share its seed-0 draw, so each removes the cases of every smaller fraction; E2 draws no cell whose population shows a concept the cell's records miss, a chance that for records drawn independently is (1 - p)^n at share p in n records (ADR `research/bko-formal-gap-definition`, Consequences); it reads one population seed with no interval; the frequency-aware reference carries no label on Synthea, so its gaps before the removals, all in age bands under 50, partly reflect incidence rising with age. Environment: Python 3.14, pronto 2.7.3, pandas 2.3.3 and scipy 1.18.1 (uv.lock). Population: run.json jar sha256 ed43c20ad40ba5c3bc724503a5af032715fe3c491620b766148e7c2361e6ecc1; seed 1, clinician seed 1, population 2000, snapshot 2026-01-01, Massachusetts; patients.csv sha256 03cbd10f710b340f05467bc674f5e6c694a9e467f67147c314f07e73d2ed5492, conditions.csv sha256 29268055a12ee7892d9e5e13366e139709fcabb7b4fba04623d2d03528faf804. BKO instantiation ran on the fused MONDO, HPO and dataset fragments (the `uci` run's population-level `fused fragments=3` line, named `uci-heart-disease`), so ADR `research/bko-formal-gap-definition`'s reversal condition did not occur; frequency-aware recovers 9 of 9 eligible `cell` pairs in `e2-4` and 3 of 37 in `e2-10`, so ADR `research/frequency-aware-gap-score`'s second reversal condition does not take it out of E1, and its rule is fixed from `e2-4`. On UCI Heart Disease, `python -m bko.gaps uci` lists no population-level gap in the training split, since every concept the field mappings name is shown by at least one training record; 16 per-subgroup pairs in 2 cells, 14 of them in the `<55`, female, target 1 cell, which holds 2 records and is one of the 2 count-based cells, and 2 in the `<55`, female, target 0 cell; 13 of the 16 are also frequency-aware gaps, the other 3 being the chain of a concept the 2-record cell does not show and the frequency-aware rule does not flag at that size; 21 frequency-aware pairs in 5 cells from 5 flagged (cell, concept) pairs, two of them with posterior mass within 0.003 of the 0.95 level (0.9527 and 0.9523); and 2 count-based cells. |

## E3. Synthea downstream augmentation

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | On a Synthea frame whose training rows lost most of one cell's cases, how much recall on that cell's held-out cases does each augmentation method recover, beside the un-ablated training split and the ablated one unaugmented? |
| Configuration | ADR [`research/synthea-downstream-design`](adr/research/synthea-downstream-design.md) holds the frame, the removal, the reference arm, the copy rule and their reasons; `bko.evaluate.experiment.E3` holds the values. Data: the Synthea population `ed43c20ad40ba5c3-s1-cs1-p2000-d20260101-massachusetts` under `data/synthea/` (the `population` line of `scores.log`), whose `run.json` reads jar sha256 `ed43c20ad40ba5c3bc724503a5af032715fe3c491620b766148e7c2361e6ecc1`, seed 1, clinician seed 1, population 2000, snapshot 2026-01-01, Massachusetts, patients.csv sha256 `03cbd10f710b340f05467bc674f5e6c694a9e467f67147c314f07e73d2ed5492` (2283 rows) and conditions.csv sha256 `29268055a12ee7892d9e5e13366e139709fcabb7b4fba04623d2d03528faf804` (211860 rows), the population [E2](#e2-synthea-gap-detection-ablation) reads; `run.json` sha256 `35a8e8fdf5a9b357c940b7a04e6087f9553b1638acfbb30be8e9885acb06129b`. Frame: the patients alive on the snapshot date and aged 40 and over, 985 records, digest `f9c6a23e30036a12a8647903783cac6cd47eac3f58e40d82b09b7d572898b94f`, from the `data dataset=synthea frame=synthea records=985 digest=` line of the rehearsal `e3-1`'s `scores.log`; the ADR's [C12] command prints the same digest at this entry's commit. Release: MONDO as `bko.datasets.sources` pins it; each slice's `scores.log` reads its `snapshot present mondo` line with `sha256=2631e8e165d41def1a46749adb1ce0a336c8aba7f6bc0978729592cee8fed60b` and `release mondo data_version=releases/2026-10-06 snapshot=2631e8e165d41def terms=63329 obsolete=4639`. Split: E1's cell-stratified split at holdout seeds 0 to 99. Removal (`E3.removal`): 0.9 of cell `(>=65, female, 1)`'s training rows leave each holdout's training split, 47 of 52, and the test split is never thinned. Arms (`E3.arms`): `none`, `class-weight`, `smote`, `adasyn`, `ctgan`, `tvae`, `bko`, `bko-population`, `bko-frequency` and `real`, which adds the removed rows back, so its training set is the un-ablated split. Replica arms (`E3.replica_arms`): the row-adding methods (`ADDS_ROWS`) and the three BKO arms. Generator: `bko.generate.messages.SETTINGS`, `Settings(model="claude-haiku-5-5", effort="medium", max_tokens=16000, per_call=20, guidance=Level.SUMMARY)`, effort `medium` being [G4](#g4-effort-at-summary)'s choice, for all three BKO arms. Prompt: `bko.generate.prompt` at level `summary` over the Synthea frame; the ADR's Egress says what each call sends. Library versions: `uv.lock` at the run commit, which each slice's `versions` line records. Criteria: none (`E3.criteria` is empty). Copies: counted, not removed (`E3.decontaminate` is false): a test record an arm copies stays in the test split every arm is scored on, each holdout writes a `copies-kept` held-out row, and `compare` pairs with `allow_copies` (the ADR, Decision). Slices: the named runs `e3-a` (holdouts 0 to 49) and `e3-b` (holdouts 50 to 99), each with a ceiling of $32.50 (`E3_SLICE_CEILING`), 2 x $32.50 = $65.00 (`E3.spend_ceiling`), launched concurrently under the paid-run protocol ([DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation)) with `--in-flight 8` each, as run ids `e3-5` (`e3-a`) and `e3-6` (`e3-b`) at tag `run/rerun-1`, which supersede `e3-2` and `e3-3` (Runs); `merge e3` joins their `scores.csv` files in seed order into the bytes one `e3` run writes and writes them to stdout, which `runs/e3-7/scores.csv` holds by redirect, and `compare e3` reads the merged file. Go/no-go: the ADR re-chooses the removal before the paid run if the rehearsal's paired `none` minus `real` recall at scope `flagged-subgroups` has an interval that includes 0 (Reversal Conditions); the rehearsal `e3-1` reads -0.3515 [-0.4819, -0.2212] at confidence 0.95 over its 100 holdouts, so the removal stands. The source is the ADR's [C13] run over `runs/e3-1/rehearsal/scores.csv` at `44aaa9e`'s code, its `none vs real augmented recall flagged-subgroups` line; at this entry's commit the same figure, negated, is the `unjudged criterion=secondary scope=flagged-subgroups arm=real against=none measure=recall` line of `compare e3 runs/e3-1/rehearsal/scores.csv --candidate real`, mean 0.351538, low 0.221202, high 0.481875 (the ADR, Rationale, The rehearsal), and that command reruns it at this entry's commit; [C13] itself pairs none of `e3-1`'s holdouts at this entry's commit, since `pairable` with `allow_copies` needs the `copies-kept` rows `e3-1` lacks. Rehearsal: `e3-1` ran at commit `44aaa9e7e9877163433c698a77adefff1762b366` on `feat/synthea-frame` (kept in PR #56's history); its folder of record is `runs/e3-1/rehearsal/` (Runs). Its `scores.csv` holds no `copies-kept` row, its `scores.log` no `removal` or `flagged` line, and its `experiment` line no `seeds`, `removal` or `decontaminate` field; the run commit's code writes all of them. Each paid slice rehearses at its run commit before it launches (`launch <run-id> <run> --rehearse`).<br>Readings: `compare e3`'s comparators are E1's, `METHOD_ARMS`, so its own contrasts never read `real`. The headline comes from three commands at the run commit, and Result quotes their lines: (i) `bko` paired against `real`, `test ! -e runs/e3-7/criteria.csv && test ! -e runs/e3-7/criteria.log && uv run --no-sync python -m bko.evaluate compare e3 runs/e3-7/scores.csv --candidate bko --beside runs/e3-7/scores.csv --beside-arm real > runs/e3-7/criteria.csv 2> runs/e3-7/criteria.log`, whose `unjudged` lines also read `bko` against each comparison method, `none` included; (ii) every arm paired against `real` and against `none` over every series `pair` pairs, the ADR's [C13] over `runs/e3-7/scores.csv`, its stdout redirected to `scratchpad/rerun/e3/c13.txt` (gitignored working output; Runs records its sha256); (iii) each arm's mean headline recall, `none`, `class-weight` and `real` included, the ADR's [C14] over `runs/e3-7/scores.csv`, its stdout redirected to `scratchpad/rerun/e3/c14.txt`. Runs quotes each as the rerun ran it, from the frozen worktree's code. Scope: recall at `flagged-subgroups` counts only cases, and `(>=65, female)` is the only flagged subgroup, so it is the recall on the removed cell's test cases; AUC-PR at that scope covers the subgroup's test records of both labels |

The 3 undecoded records come from three short calls (`WARNING bko.generate.calls: short` lines: `e3-2`'s calls 3227 and 5288, `e3-3`'s call 5833, 1 each). `decoded` counts every item a reply returns (`bko.generate.calls`, `bko.generate.decode`), so it runs above `asked` where replies return surplus items: 361 of `e3-2`'s calls returned 444 items beyond their ask and 374 of `e3-3`'s returned 470 (113843 decoded = 113401 asked - 2 undecoded + 444; 113867 = 113398 - 1 + 470); the canned rehearsals return none, and `e1-4`'s call log reads the same rule (77489 decoded against 77487 asked). `asked` runs 1 above the 113400 quota in `e3-2` and 2 below in `e3-3`: each short call's one-record top-up adds one (the `guided` lines asking 16 and 742), and an attempt whose earlier replies' surplus records counted toward a cell before that cell's call asks one fewer (the `guided` lines asking 740: 1 in `e3-2`, 3 in `e3-3`). The harness retried tvae's fill, a local synthesizer making no API call: `tvae` and `tvae-replica` on holdouts 14 and 26 of `e3-2` and 76 of `e3-3`, `tvae-replica` alone on holdouts 4, 60 and 81; each slice retried `tvae` 2 times and `tvae-replica` 6 times, each recovered (the `WARNING bko.evaluate.run: retry` lines). Each slice's 50 holdouts log `removal holdout=<n> cell=>=65/female/1 fraction=0.9 train=788 removed=47 kept=741` and `flagged holdout=<n> cells=>=65/female/1`, and each `test holdout=` line reads `records=197` and `kept=true`, with `copied` 152 to 177 over `e3-2`'s lines and 156 to 177 over `e3-3`'s, against 146 to 167 over the rehearsal `e3-1`'s and 146 to 167 and 147 to 165 over the slices' own rehearsals. The estimate overstated on both sides: input ran 139.800 and 137.573 tokens per record asked against the basis's 300.535, output 152.358 and 152.942 against 211.162; output is $8.6388 of `e3-2`'s $10.2241 (84.5%) and $8.6717 of `e3-3`'s $10.2317 (84.8%). (ii) [C13] wrote `scratchpad/e3-paired.txt` (gitignored), 79 lines, sha256 `26516c561bb65fc3aff4fc5699e84a450480f7bd5183eb111a126d80f194ad03`; (iii) [C14] wrote `scratchpad/e3-means.txt` (gitignored), 10 lines, sha256 `b0f0c3aaae3c3d5922280aa1e5a9f3d918cc3d4e9a9ad738485b7702d7650a7d` · `scratchpad/e3-4-merge.log` and `runs/e3-4/criteria.log` carry a wall-clock stamp on every line, so a re-run matches them by the content after each stamp, not by sha256 · no API call, no API spend<br>Spend estimate, stated before `e3-2` and `e3-3` (ceilings 2 x $32.50): the basis is `e1-4`, the judged run at `SETTINGS` ([E1](#e1-uci-heart-disease-augmentation) Runs row), whose call log's `end` line reads `"usd": 10.509915100000024` over `"asked": 77487`, so a record asked costs 10.509915100000024 / 77487 = $1.356346e-4 (its 23287576 input and 16362315 output tokens are 300.535 and 211.162 per record asked, at $0.10 and $0.50 per million, `bko.generate.spend.PRICES`; output is $8.1812 of the $10.5099, 77.8%). Per holdout each BKO arm asks 756 records: a replica of the thinned training split, 788 - 47 = 741 rows, plus the removed cell's 15-row fill. 3 arms x 756 records x 100 holdouts = 226800 records asked, and 226800 x $1.356346e-4 = $30.76; each slice asks 3 x 756 x 50 = 113400, half of it, $15.38, under its $32.50 ceiling. The rehearsal `e3-1`'s call log asked 226800, this quota, in 12900 calls, 129 per holdout and 6450 per slice. The basis likely overstates on the input side: `e3-1`'s requests hold 59700831 characters of system prompt and user turn over 226800 records asked, 263.23 per record asked, against `e1-4`'s 44714267 over 77487, 577.06, since a Synthea record has 9 fields against UCI's 14 and its calls ask 17.58 records each against `e1-4`'s 14.11. Output, 77.8% of `e1-4`'s spend, need not shrink with the fields, since the thinking at effort `medium` is part of it. Top-ups and refused attempts' retries add to it (`e1-4` asked 77487 against a base quota of 77400). Worst case: an attempt asks at most `E3.budget` = 3 times its rows, so a slice's hard maximum before retries is 3 x $15.38 = $46.14, above its $32.50 ceiling, which stops the slice first, as the projection after 5 holdouts does for any slice projected to reach it.<br>Duration estimate for `e3-2` and `e3-3`, a bracket per slice, the two slices running concurrently. Ceiling side: `e1-4`'s call log runs from its `run` line's `started` 2026-10-28T08:08:12.860632Z to its `end` line's `ended` 13:08:18.644358Z, 18005.784 s, for 77487 records asked at 8 in flight, 0.2323717 s per record asked; 113400 x 0.2323717 s = 26351 s, about 7.3 h. That rate measures `e1-4`'s wave packing, not throughput at 8 in flight: its mean in flight is 3.59, and 41% of its span ran with one call in flight ([E1](#e1-uci-heart-disease-augmentation) Runs row). Full packing: 6450 calls x `e1-4`'s mean call latency 11.772 s (request `at` to reply `at`, 5490 calls) / 8 in flight = 9491 s, about 2.6 h; E3's calls ask more records each, and live top-ups add calls. High side: G4 is the measured precedent for two concurrent runs at 8 in flight each sharing the API: `g4-1` and `g4-2` started 2 s apart (05:07:57Z and 05:07:59Z), both call logs' `end` lines read `rate_limited` false and `retried` 0, and `g4-2`'s mean call latency was 11.491 s over 1840 calls against `e1-4`'s 11.772 s; `g4-2`'s wall time, 6784.346 s for 25829 records asked, 0.2626639 s per record asked, gives 113400 x 0.2626639 s = 29786 s, about 8.3 h. The account's rate limit is not known, so G4 supports, without bounding, two slices at 8 in flight each. Local scoring: the rehearsal `e3-1` scored all 100 holdouts on canned replies in 884 s (21:20:10 to 21:34:54 EDT, its `launch.log`), about 9 s per holdout, about 7.4 min per 50-holdout slice alone; the two slices share the CPU, so each takes longer beside the other. `e1-4`'s wall time already holds its own local work, on UCI's training rows, and E3's local arms fit about three times as many (741 against 238), which the scaling does not count (the ADR, Rationale).<br>Merge: PR #57 merged as squash commit `fc9d92e`, and `git diff --stat 8a50dc6 fc9d92e -- src` prints nothing.<br>Shared by the | Read against | Measures with no verdict: ADR [`research/synthea-downstream-design`](adr/research/synthea-downstream-design.md) sets no criterion. The headline, recall at scope `flagged-subgroups` (the removed cell's held-out cases; AUC-PR at that scope covers the subgroup's test records of both labels), from Configuration's three readings over the rerun `e3-7`, reads each arm paired, in-run and on the same holdouts, against `real` (the un-ablated training split) and `none` (the ablated training split, unaugmented); [RESEARCH.md: intended users and evaluation setting](RESEARCH.md#2-intended-users-and-evaluation-setting) states the experiment; verdict: reported, no criterion applies (`runs/e3-7/criteria.csv` holds its header alone). All intervals below are LR's, from [C13] and reading (i) (Result), each at confidence 0.95 on its own; Multiplicity says which survive an adjustment.<br>Headline: every row-adding method and every BKO arm recovers part of the removed cell's recall over `none` and stays below `real`: each interval against `none` excludes 0 and is positive (+0.109231 for `smote` to +0.142308 for `bko-frequency`), and each against `real` excludes 0 and is negative (-0.242308 for `smote` to -0.209231 for `bko-frequency`). Reading (i) pairs `bko` with each method directly: its recall intervals against `smote`, `adasyn`, `ctgan` and `tvae` contain 0 at `flagged-subgroups` and at `all`, so the intervals resolve no difference between `bko` and the four row-adding methods; at `flagged-subgroups` they leave differences from +0.089358 (against `smote`) to -0.070888 (against `tvae`) possible. Against `class-weight` its recall intervals exclude 0 and are negative (-0.416154 [-0.547387, -0.284921] at `flagged-subgroups`), while its AUC-PR intervals contain 0.<br>Where the gain lands: a holdout's test split holds 13 removed-cell cases and 52 cases in all (Result, unregistered check (d)), so a recall gain over `none` times 13 or 52 is the mean count of cases gained. The removed cell's share of all cases gained runs from 78% (`bko`, 1.74 of 2.22) to 102% (`tvae`, 1.83 of 1.80) over the row-adding and BKO arms, and is 85% for `real` (4.57 of 5.37) and 48% for `class-weight` (7.15 of 14.77): the row-adding arms and `real` move mostly the removed cell's cases, and `class-weight` moves cases in every cell.<br>`class-weight` reaches 0.8123, above `real` (+0.198462 [+0.090648, +0.306275]), and its recall at `all` rises most over `none` (+0.284038 [+0.229680, +0.338397], against at most +0.044038 for any other augmenting arm and +0.103269 for `real`). It pays in precision: LR's mean precision at `all` is 0.5629 against `none`'s 0.8002, `real`'s 0.7600 and `bko`'s 0.7759, and 0.5976 against `none`'s 0.7020 at `flagged-subgroups`; the unregistered paired interval, `class-weight` minus `none` at `all`, reads -0.237241 [-0.295688, -0.178793] (Result, check (c)). Its AUC-PR at `all` moves +0.008978 [+0.001548, +0.016408], at most +0.016 against recall's +0.284, and its AUC-PR interval at `flagged-subgroups` contains 0.<br>AUC-PR at `flagged-subgroups`: no interval resolves a move for any arm, `real` included. Every interval against `none` and against `real` contains 0, with half-widths 0.008796 to 0.020972, and `real` against `none` reads -0.003476 [-0.018673, +0.011721]. Under LR, the removal lowers the cell's recall at the default threshold (`real` against `none` +0.351538 [+0.221202, +0.481875]) with no change in ranking within the subgroup that the intervals resolve: the removal destroys the cell's base rate in training, so the recovery E3 measures under LR is a subgroup-level score shift across the threshold, not better ranking of the subgroup's cases above its non-cases. That is why, under LR, the headline ranks `class-weight`, which adds no rows, first. At `all`, `real` against `none` excludes 0 narrowly (+0.013524 [+0.001050, +0.025998]), as do `class-weight`, `ctgan` and the three BKO arms against `none`; the intervals of `smote`, `adasyn` and `tvae` contain 0.<br>HGB: the registered readings pair no HGB series, so the ranking of `class-weight` first holds under LR alone. HGB's mean recall at `flagged-subgroups` is `none` 0.090769, `class-weight` 0.129231, the BKO arms 0.308462 to 0.321538 and `real` 0.548462 (`mean` lines), means only. The one unregistered HGB interval, `real` minus `none` AUC-PR at `flagged-subgroups`, +0.077647 [-0.049797, +0.205092] (Result, check (c)), is too wide to read either way.<br>Privacy: the `dcr-p5` intervals against `real` contain 0 for every arm but `adasyn` (-0.002154 [-0.003233, -0.001076]). Reading (i)'s `bko` against `none` (+0.003135 [+0.000677, +0.005593]) compares `bko`'s added records' `dcr-p5` with `none`'s held-out `dcr-p5`, 0.0 on every holdout, so it is no arm contrast. Reported with no verdict, since the frame's records repeat (the ADR, Consequences).<br>Copies: no arm is scored on a copy-free test split. On every holdout 156 to 178 of the 197 test records equal some arm's added or replica row (the `test` lines' `copied=`), and the arms' added rows copy different numbers of test records (`copies` lines, summed over the holdouts: `bko` 76, `bko-population` 88, `bko-frequency` 92, `smote` 74, `adasyn` 60, `ctgan` 69, `tvae` 158, `real` 288).<br>Multiplicity: E3 reads 104 intervals, [C13]'s 79 and reading (i)'s 25, 96 of them distinct ([C13]'s 4 `real vs none` lines mirror its `none vs real` lines, and reading (i)'s 4 `bko` against `none` utility lines repeat [C13]'s). With no arm differing, about 5 of the 96 (0.05 x 96 = 4.8) would exclude 0 by chance. Holm's step-down at family-wise 0.05 over the corrected t's two-sided p-values (Result, check (a)): over the 18 headline intervals, recall at `flagged-subgroups` against `real` and against `none`, all 18 survive (largest adjusted p 0.0236). Over the 96 distinct intervals, 23 of the 44 that exclude 0 survive: every recall interval against `real`, at both scopes; `class-weight` against `none` and `bko` against `class-weight`, recall at both scopes; and `adasyn`'s `dcr-p5` against `real` (adjusted p 0.011). The row-adding and BKO arms' recall intervals against `none`, at both scopes (adjusted p 0.107 to 1), every AUC-PR exclusion at `all` (0.734 to 1) and reading (i)'s `dcr-p5` against `none` (0.804) do not survive over the 96.<br>The contrasts are discrete, recall moving in steps of 1/13 at `flagged-subgroups`, and reading (i)'s four row-adding contrasts there differ on 34 to 41 of 100 holdouts (`differing=`); the corrected t's coverage on such tied data is untested. One run, one sample of holdouts, no criterion and no verdict; the readings show no more than these intervals. |

Only target 80's overall AUC-PR interval excludes 0, below target 20. More rows move recall up and overall ranking down, as a classifier's threshold moving toward the added positives would; no reading here measures the threshold itself |
| Read against | [RESEARCH.md section 4](RESEARCH.md#4-success-criteria); verdict: reported, no criterion applies. The criteria read the judged configuration at its threshold and target |

## E5. Augmentation against training-set size

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | With the test split fixed, does augmentation pay more when each holdout keeps only a quarter, half or three quarters of each cell's training rows, for recall and AUC-PR over the whole test split and the flagged subgroups? |
| Configuration | Runs: `e5-quarter`, `e5-half` and `e5-threequarters` in `bko.evaluate.named_runs.RUNS` (`E5_FRACTIONS`), each `SETTINGS_SWEEP` (E1's holdouts, split, methods and `bko`, replica arms the row-adding methods and `bko`, threshold and fill target 20, a $10.00 ceiling, no criterion) with `training_fraction` 1/4, 1/2 or 3/4, at `SETTINGS`; each holdout keeps per cell the fraction of its training rows rounded half up, never below 2, the test split whole (ADR [`research/e1-evaluation-protocol`](adr/research/e1-evaluation-protocol.md), A training fraction). The held-out ratio reads the subsample, so each interval widens as rows leave. `e1-5` is the fraction-1 point. Two things move with the fraction besides the rows: more cells fall under the threshold, so the flagged scope grows (the headline reads scope `all`), and those cells ask more gap rows, so the curve also carries dose. Over E1's 100 holdouts, from `bko.evaluate.prepare.prepared` and the gap request on the UCI snapshot: fraction 1/4 flags all 8 cells on every holdout, asks 9700 gap rows and 6300 replica rows, largest held-out ratio 1.0; 1/2 flags 6, asks 5200 and 12100, ratio 0.5556; 3/4 flags 3, asks 2600 and 18000, ratio 0.3571. Estimate, before the runs: `e1-4`'s `bko` spend per gap row ($0.2879 / 2000) and per replica row ($3.1911 / 23822) times the rows asked: $2.24 (`e5-quarter`), $2.37 (`e5-half`), $2.79 (`e5-threequarters`). Readings: `compare <run> <scores> --candidate bko` into `<run-id>-judged`, whose `unjudged` lines carry each contrast of `bko` against each method with its corrected interval and no verdict; a reading beside `e1-5` pairs no holdout, since a subsample changes every holdout's held-out ratio and flagged cells (`runs/e5-4-beside`: `paired=0 excluded=100 copied=1 mismatched=99`, refused), so each fraction reads within its own run and `e1-5`'s criteria are the fraction-1 point |

| Result | Over 100 holdouts (`mean` and `unjudged` lines of each `criteria.log`; fraction 1 is `runs/e1-5/criteria.log`). Whole test split, `none` then `bko`: recall 0.7915 then 0.7748 (1/4), 0.7978 then 0.8048 (1/2), 0.8104 then 0.8163 (3/4), 0.8033 then 0.8092 (1); AUC-PR 0.8930 then 0.8792, 0.9030 then 0.8994, 0.9098 then 0.9058, 0.9118 then 0.9101. `bko` minus `none`, mean [corrected interval]: recall -0.0167 [-0.1484, +0.1150] (1/4), +0.0070 [-0.0749, +0.0889] (1/2), +0.0059 [-0.0502, +0.0621] (3/4), +0.0059 [-0.0320, +0.0438] (1); AUC-PR -0.0138 [-0.0804, +0.0527], -0.0036 [-0.0346, +0.0273], -0.0040 [-0.0177, +0.0097], -0.0018 [-0.0119, +0.0084]. Every other contrast of `bko` against a method, at every fraction and both scopes, holds 0 too: at 1/2 the 12 means lie between -0.0126 and +0.0141; at 3/4 the 12 primary means lie between -0.0104 and +0.0083 and the 12 flagged-subgroup means between -0.0311 and +0.0076 (`runs/e5-6-judged/criteria.log`). At 1/4 and 1/2 the flagged cells cover every age-sex subgroup, so the flagged scope is the whole split; at 3/4 `bko` minus `none` on the flagged subgroups reads recall -0.0226 [-0.0804, +0.0351] and AUC-PR -0.0080 [-0.0246, +0.0085]. `bko` validity: 16011 of 16013 (1/4), 17316 of 17316 (1/2), 20642 of 20643 (3/4) passed, with 11, 12 and 42 quota drops and 4 cell drops at 1/2. Augmentation does not pay more as rows leave: the intervals widen with the held-out ratio, and every mean stays near 0, below it on AUC-PR at every fraction |
| Read against | [RESEARCH.md section 4](RESEARCH.md#4-success-criteria); verdict: reported, no criterion applies |

## E6. Diabetes 130 subgroup removal

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | On real inpatient encounters, with 0.985 and then all of one cell's training positives removed, how much of the removed cell's recall, and of AUC-PR over the whole test split, does each augmentation method recover against `none` and the `real` reference? |
| Configuration | Data: UCI Diabetes 130-US Hospitals as `bko.datasets.sources` pins it (`uci-diabetes-130`, sha256 `f82ac129da2ddd2299391ff6fbae3a6a58b3edcf59ac9d7bd480c00fe453112a`), the frame `bko.datasets.diabetes.frame_records` builds; frame digest `38b599c950d6f7bf70620bb8e0fbe90888de56fedf55d5dbb93aa1a06e9ab0cb` (records 69283), the `data dataset=diabetes frame=diabetes records=69283 digest=38b599c950d6f7bf70620bb8e0fbe90888de56fedf55d5dbb93aa1a06e9ab0cb agreement=none` line `load` logs. Design: ADR [`research/diabetes-subgroup-removal-design`](adr/research/diabetes-subgroup-removal-design.md) holds the cohort, label, cutoff, cell, removal, fill, arms, replica, holdouts and family, and its reasons. Runs: `e6-thin` (f = 0.985) and `e6-empty` (1.0) in `bko.evaluate.named_runs`, over the 16 runnable arms of the ADR's Decision, Arms (every arm but the curated `cllm`), 14 comparators, each K = 10 slices of 10 consecutive holdouts, `e6-thina` (holdouts 0 to 9) to `e6-thinj` (90 to 99) and `e6-emptya` to `e6-emptyj`, all 20 run at once at 4 calls in flight per slice (the ADR's Decision, Slices and In flight). Settings: `E6_SETTINGS`, `SETTINGS` with `max_tokens` 32000. Pilots (live, the Diabetes case of `tests/test_generate_live.py`, invented guidance for `(6-7, male, 1)`, 20 records asked per call, 4,015 input tokens per call): `e6-1` at 16000 tokens stopped at its call 5 of 5 on `max_tokens` (16,000 output tokens, undecodable), 80 of 100 records decoded, 200.8 input and 590.6 output tokens per record asked, $3.1536e-4 per record asked (`runs/e6-1/pilot.log`); `e6-1b-16000`, 10 calls at 16000: 0 stopped at the limit, 198 of 200 records decoded, output 10,563 to 12,653 tokens per call, mean 11,308; `e6-1b-32000`, 10 calls at 32000: 0 stopped at the limit, 199 of 200 decoded, output 7,818 to 13,352, mean 10,909. At 16000, 1 of 15 calls stopped at the limit; at 32000, 0 of 10. `e6-1c` (2026-11-02, commit `a2d82aa8595d0d537dc9ee773372be608d27939f`, `E6_THIN` with arms `none` and `cllm`, holdouts 0 and 1, ceiling $15): the curated `cllm` asked 46,360 records (holdout 0: 26,460 over calls 1 to 1323, kept 0, 26,389 dropped by curation; holdout 1: 19,900 over calls 1324 to 2318, kept 0, 19,757 dropped by curation), spent $15.0042 over 2,318 calls and stopped on the ceiling (`end` line `status=failed stop=ceiling`, 1 `max_tokens` stop), $3.24e-4 per record asked; the curator rejects every generated record of the removed cell, so at budget 22 the arm would spend about $1,700 for zero rows and E6 does not run it; `runs/e6-1c/calls.jsonl` sha256 `9b90a7fb0dd743eefaf8d6fc02d001740cd0d5258888a81f23e01eccdaa5d6ec`, `scores.log` sha256 `6000319b43da6ad874d6b0c5e49e2c5dfb1868fccefdd9e3c6791786cb41dcff`. Rate limits (response headers): requests 45,000 a minute, output tokens 2,000,000 a minute, input tokens 10,000,000 a minute. Estimate: rate per record asked, the larger of `e1-4`'s $1.35635e-4 x 34 / 14 columns = $3.2940e-4 and `e6-1`'s $3.1536e-4, so $3.2940e-4; drop share 0.0075 (3 of 400, the two 10-call pilots pooled: 2 of 200 at 16000 and 1 of 200 at 32000); records asked per holdout, with R the cell's kept training rows (19 at f = 0.985, 0 at 1.0): the five BKO-type arms and `cllm-uncurated` 6 x (1269 - R) x 1.0075, and at f = 0.985 `bko`'s replica 2000 x 1.0075, so 9,571 at f = 0.985 (6 x 1250 x 1.0075 + 2000 x 1.0075) and 7,671 at 1.0 (6 x 1269 x 1.0075); over 100 holdouts, $315.27 and $252.68, $567.95 in all. Ceilings: the $1,500 cap split in proportion to records asked, `e6-thin` 1500 x 9,571 / 17,242 = $832.65 and `e6-empty` $667.35, 2.6411 times each estimate; each slice a tenth, $83.26 and $66.74 (`test_each_e6_ceiling_covers_its_estimate`). <br>Merge: PR #72 merged as squash commit `22a58c9`. The pilots ran before it: `e6-1` and the two 10-call pilots at `1eef757`, `e6-1c` at `a2d82aa`; `git diff --stat 1eef757 22a58c9 -- src` lists 23 files and `git diff --stat a2d82aa 22a58c9 -- src` 19, PR #72's later commits (the resume guard, the review's fixes and the named runs), so the pilots' figures are readings of their own commits. |

(30,100 rows, 100 holdouts); `e6-23` (`e6-empty`, `e6-12` to `e6-21`), superseded by `e6-47` (rerun at `run/rerun-1`), `runs/e6-23/scores.csv` sha256 Readings at the run commit, superseded by `e6-46-judged` and `e6-47-judged` (rerun at `run/rerun-1`), `compare <run> runs/e6-2x/scores.csv --candidate <arm>` into `runs/e6-2x-judged/criteria-<arm>.{csv,log}`, exit 0 each, for `bko` and `bko-direct`, and at f = 0.985 for `real`, the ADR's G3 reversal check (`criteria-real.log` sha256 `8324211b80c98fbd6f454e6b01280395527ac4c4d4e93f271ac46854f18f553f`); the csv holds only its header (no criterion of RESEARCH section 4 is read for E6), the logs' sha256 `e6-22` `bko` `28845b297d3d893f343d3fe4f7e9dce65af35879dbb59134527f56a4ce849fc0`, `bko-direct` `5f2e3649771492222c237804120f090d0209b78e109bf52651c5589e6a13c307`; `e6-23` `bko` `2255ed44ff1363246255576cfd8fc2e8af0fb8d33b38316926a19eb09de75263`, `bko-direct` `f3c200b0845202e44e3023d2a6aa52428ef6bacb9af26b9f4e63d7bb16d13275`. <br>Recorded by PR #74, merged as squash commit `f42ba4f5b5a109c6833aae53ba47dc9d9ca7dab3`. <br>Replays: `e6-2` and `e6-12` replay at the run commit, free (`score <slice> --replay runs/e6-<k>/calls.jsonl` under `env -u ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and the same thread settings), into `runs/e6-2-replay/` and `runs/e6-12-replay/`. `e6-12`'s replay, superseded by `e6-35-replay` (rerun at `run/rerun-1`) (commit `22a58c9`, `dirty=false`, 8 calls in flight; its `run` line's `replay` the paid log's sha256 `08fff1437e92032b87f350f5d1007334ac2c7598e81a70a6a46691130265a505`; its own `calls.jsonl` sha256 `710d574c17a356457fc8720e979e95a0d2f277872c921df75a36b3b948f18112`) ends `complete` at 2026-11-05T04:12Z, `live_usd` 0.0, repeating the paid run's 3,239 calls, 63,752 records asked and 63,609 decoded; `cmp` of its `scores.csv` and `holdout-candidates.jsonl` with `runs/e6-12/`'s: equal, both. `e6-2`'s replay, superseded by `e6-25-replay` (rerun at `run/rerun-1`) (the same commit, `dirty=false`, 8 calls in flight; `replay` the paid log's sha256 `ab88d9a6ec34f4fdd23c72afb88e4532fbf1726410e384120be490ed0646505f`; its own `calls.jsonl` sha256 `6454612b782294d3fafaca9c57abe89b1f6e6c79b4f06cbf81519ead2031c9ec`) ends `complete` at 2026-11-05T10:59Z, `live_usd` 0.0, repeating the paid run's 4,293 calls, 82,874 records asked and 82,741 decoded; `cmp` of its `holdout-candidates.jsonl` with `runs/e6-2/`'s: equal. Its `scores.csv` (sha256 `d59cb8acd59e545c93852dfb1d57114ad7f9aa9aa456cbe98b7ecf935aed9d75`) differs from the paid run's in 20 of 3,010 rows, all `bko`'s replica `quality` and `column-pair-trends`, at most 0.000036 apart; every other row is equal. The cause is SDMetrics' quality report: it subsamples each table to 50,000 rows, unseeded, for its contingency column pairs when either is larger, and the f = 0.985 training split holds 54,177 rows, so `e6-thin`'s replica `quality` and `column-pair-trends`, like any fidelity reading of a Diabetes 130 training split made before the fix, are each one draw of that subsample. `bko.evaluate.fidelity` now makes the report read every row, so a reading on Diabetes 130 replays exactly; the recorded `e6-thin` replica values stay as run. <br>Shared by the rerun's 20 slices `e6-25` to `e6-44`, their merges `e6-46` and `e6-47`, their readings and the replays `e6-25-replay` and `e6-35-replay`: tag `run/rerun-1`, commit `2e80bd89355e9a1f4566b75a145acac6fd3fddb5` (each `versions` line, `dirty=false`); `M` is `<repo>` (the repository) and `W` is `<worktree>` (a worktree detached at the tag); each command as `scratchpad/rerun/wave2/launch.sh`, `replays.sh` or `readings.sh` (gitignored) runs it, guarded by `test ! -e` on each output, their output in `launch.out`, `replays.out` and `readings.out` beside them. The 20 slices and `e6-curated` ([G7](#g7-bkb-likelihood-curation)) launched together under the paid-run protocol ([DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation)), with no rehearsal as recorded, on 2026-11-09 from 14:55:36 to 15:00:20 EST (`launch.out`), once every Wave 1 launch but `b3-synthea` had ended (the script's precondition), each as `env VECLIB_MAXIMUM_THREADS=1 OMP_NUM_THREADS=1 PYTHONPATH=$W/src $M/.venv/bin/python -m bko.evaluate launch e6-<k> <slice> --in-flight 4 --runs-dir $M/runs --data-dir $M/data </dev/null`, into `runs/e6-<k>/` (no move); exit 0 each (`launch e6-<k> <slice> exit=0 (expect 0)` in `launch.out`); each `launch.log` reads `power=mains` and `awake=caffeinate`. No slice's supervisor stopped, resumed or started a second segment; each `scores.log` holds `run status=complete` and no `stop rule=` line. The call log's `run` line: `settings` model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `medium`, `max_tokens` 32000, `per_call` 20, `guidance` `summary`; `in_flight` 4; served model `claude-haiku-5-5` on every reply (each reply's `model`). Within the slices, calls retried by the client, from each `end` line's `retried`: 11 at f = 0.985 and 6 at 1.0; replies stopped at `max_tokens` (`stop_reason=max_tokens` in `scores.log`, their records undecodable): 28 at f = 0.985 and 26 at 1.0. `decoded` can exceed `asked`: a reply can hold more records than its call asked (2,280 `generate` lines at f = 0.985 and 2,253 at 1.0 read `decoded` above `asked`), so `e6-37`'s and `e6-43`'s totals decode more than they ask. <br>`e6-25` (`e6-thina`) · started 2026-11-09T19:55Z, ended 2026-11-11T12:09Z · `supervise done segments=1 last=e6-25 | Result | Summary. Among the arms that score a non-degenerate recall (`class-weight`'s is every test record scored positive at the 0.0902 cutoff, so its recall reads nothing; its AUC-PR does), `bko-direct` has the highest removed-cell recall but `real`'s at 1.0 (0.5377 against 0.5565), above CTGAN by +0.0595 [+0.0115, +0.1075], with AUC-PR on the flagged subgroups level with `none` (-0.0017 [-0.0042, +0.0008]). At 1.0 SMOTE, ADASYN, TVAE and CART add no rows of the removed cell and the Bayesian network adds 1 to 4 rows on 77 holdouts and none on the rest; all five score as `none` to within 0.0002. At f = 0.985, `bko-direct` (0.4891) is level with ADASYN (+0.0058 [-0.0140, +0.0257]) and CTGAN (+0.0124 [-0.0383, +0.0630]) and above SMOTE (+0.0627 [+0.0134, +0.1121]); ADASYN's added rows' 5th-percentile distance to the training rows is 0.0000 against `bko-direct`'s 0.0194. `bko` against `cllm-uncurated`: removed-cell recall +0.0253 [+0.0038, +0.0468] at 1.0 and +0.0139 [-0.0177, +0.0455] at f = 0.985; recall over all records +0.0187 [+0.0057, +0.0317] and +0.0187 [+0.0001, +0.0374]; AUC-PR lower at both scopes and both fractions (flagged -0.0057 [-0.0102, -0.0012] and -0.0101 [-0.0159, -0.0043]). `bko` trails CTGAN on removed-cell recall at both fractions. `bko-direct` minus CTGAN in the added rows' 5th-percentile distance at 1.0, a plain paired interval over 100 holdouts: +0.0030 [+0.0026, +0.0035]. The ADR's reversal conditions both read as not met: at f = 0.985 `real` minus `none` on removed-cell recall is +0.4878 [+0.4625, +0.5132], so `none - real` has its upper bound below 0 and |D| above 0.1609; the measured cost per record asked is $3.161e-4 ($463.6219 over 1,466,752 records asked), below 1.25 times the $3.2940e-4 estimate. Against the superseded `e6-22` and `e6-23`, every arm that calls no API (the classical arms, the synthesizers, `real`, `none` and `bko-direct`) reads the same means to every digit; the five API arms' LR and HGB means moved by at most 0.0046 (`bko`'s removed-cell recall at f = 0.985, 0.3321 to 0.3275). <br>**e6-thin (f = 0.985), `runs/e6-46/scores.csv` over 100 holdouts.** Means, LR, as recall flagged / all, AUC-PR flagged / all, then HGB recall flagged: `real` 0.5565 / 0.5299, 0.1558 / 0.1406, 0.5853; ## E7. Classifier panel

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | Under a random forest, extra trees, a multilayer perceptron and naive Bayes beside the regression and the boosting model, how do E6's arms, E1's arms and E6's curated arm rank on recall and AUC-PR, how calibrated is each fit, and do `bko-direct`'s and `bko`'s contrasts hold across models? |
| Configuration | Design: ADR [`research/classifier-panel`](adr/research/classifier-panel.md) fixes the four added models (`rf`, `et`, `mlp`, `nb`, `bko.evaluate.classifier.PANEL`), their settings, the 15 fixed perceptron epochs, the calibration measures (`brier`, `calibration-in-the-large`) and the two stages; each panel model and measure is reported, none judged. Read folders: E1's judged `e1-5` (run `e1`, [E1](#e1-uci-heart-disease-augmentation)), E6's 20 slices `e6-25` to `e6-44` ([E6](#e6-diabetes-130-subgroup-removal)) and `e6-45` (`e6-curated`, [G7](#g7-bkb-likelihood-curation)); the superseded panels read `e1-4`, `e6-2` to `e6-21` and `e6-24`. Commands: the `rows` and `panel` commands as [DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation) gives them, then `merge` and `compare` as for E6. Models logged on every panel `experiment` line: `models=lr,hgb,rf,et,mlp,nb`. <br>**Stage 1, the rows stores** (the superseded stores first, the rerun's after them; ADR [`research/rebuilt-rows-store`](adr/research/rebuilt-rows-store.md)), which E8 also reads: `python -m bko.evaluate rows <run> --folder runs/<id> --rows runs/<id>-rows/rows.jsonl` at tag `run/e6-rows`, commit `d8870f2d2c5594c3f412390ce150e18410a96f64` (each `versions` line, `dirty=false`), from a detached worktree of it (`../synthetic-rows`, since removed) with `PYTHONPATH` at its `src` and `--data-dir` the main tree's `data/`; no API call; each `rows.jsonl` made read-only once written. Every fill line's sha256 matched its `arm` line (`matches_arm=true` throughout). The strict check 7 (ADR [`research/shared-test-split-reading`](adr/research/shared-test-split-reading.md)) passed on every holdout: each thin slice's `differing` count is its `bko` replica's `quality` and `column-pair-trends` rows, 2 per holdout, the 50,000-row exception, and its `matches_folder=false` follows from them alone; each empty slice and `e6-24` reads `matches_folder=true`; `e1-4` reads `differing` 0 and `matches_folder=false` because its folder was scored before the `flagged-fingerprint` held-out row existed, and `diff` of the two sorted `scores.csv` files lists only the store's 100 added `flagged-fingerprint` rows. No folder was dropped. The empty slices, `e7-1` and `e7-24` ran together from `scratchpad/readings-pass.sh`, the thin slices from `scratchpad/thin-pass.sh` (start and exit times from `scratchpad/readings-pass.log`). Each panel log's `panel rows=` sha256 equals its store's `rows.jsonl`, and its `shared folder=` `log_sha256` equals the read folder's `scores.log`. Every panel run passed the strict check 7: `differing` repeats stage 1's per slice, and `partial` counts the stored partial fills returned whole. Every `fit` line reads `n_iter=15 converged=false`, the fixed epochs. Compute: each `fit` line is one training set fitted by all six models, so 1,600 training sets at f = 0.985 and 1,600 at 1.0 (9,600 and 9,600 model fits), 900 for `e1-4` (5,400) and 300 for `e6-24` Against CTGAN at 1.0, `bko-direct`'s removed-cell recall is higher under LR, HGB, RF and NB (intervals above 0) and its interval holds 0 under ET and the MLP; at f = 0.985 it is lower under HGB and ET and its interval holds 0 under the other four. `bko` against `cllm-uncurated` on removed-cell recall is lower under HGB, RF, ET and the MLP at both fractions and higher under NB at both. `none`'s calibration-in-the-large on the flagged subgroups is negative under every model at both fractions (f = 0.985: -0.0528 LR to -0.0922 HGB), and `real`'s lies within 0.0075 of 0 under every model at both; `class-weight` overestimates risk over all records under LR (+0.3762 at f = 0.985). ## E8. Membership inference, NNDR and DCR on E6

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | On E6's holdouts, do the rows each arm adds let a distance-based attacker tell training records from held-out ones beyond what `real`'s rows do, and how do the rows' nearest-neighbour distance ratios and distances to the closest training record read beside the held-out baseline? |
| Configuration | Design: ADR [`research/membership-inference-reading`](adr/research/membership-inference-reading.md) fixes the attack (minus the distance to the nearest added row, under the DCR metric), members (the prepared training split) and non-members (the test split), the three scopes (outside the removed cell, the cell where the removal keeps members, label 1 outside the cell), the rates (TPR at FPR 0.01 and 0.001, a rate below one non-member's share undefined), NNDR (d1 / d2 per added row, its 5th percentile and median), `real` as the reference, and the reading rule (`leaks_more`), which is no criterion. Rows: the stage 1 stores of [E7](#e7-classifier-panel)'s Configuration, read by `python -m bko.evaluate privacy <run> --rows ...` as [DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation) gives it, the thin slices' 10 stores together, the empty slices' 10 together, and `e6-curated`'s alone (`e6-45-rows`; `e6-24-rows` for the superseded `e8-3`); each privacy log's `privacy store=` line names a store whose sha256 and folder `log_sha256` match the files. Frame, removal and holdouts are E6's ([E6](#e6-diabetes-130-subgroup-removal)). `test-copies` reads E6's merged scores, `runs/e6-46`, `runs/e6-47` and `runs/e6-45` (`runs/e6-22`, `runs/e6-23` and `runs/e6-24` for the superseded readings). |
| Runs | Shared by the three readings `e8-1` to `e8-3` (the rerun's entries below state their own): tag `run/e8-1`, commit `6f5def87cd6ac9a588051dae2137a2a4a8eeadee`, from a detached worktree of it as `nice -n 5 env VECLIB_MAXIMUM_THREADS=1 OMP_NUM_THREADS=1 PYTHONPATH=<worktree>/src <repo>/.venv/bin/python -P <repo>/scratchpad/main-with-runs.py privacy <run> --rows <repo>/runs/<id>-rows/rows.jsonl ... --data-dir <repo>/data`, stdout to `runs/e8-<k>/privacy.csv` and stderr to `runs/e8-<k>/privacy.log`; free, no API call. The wrapper calls `bko.evaluate.__main__.main` with `Context(runs_dir=<repo>/runs)` after `configure_logging()`, because `privacy` resolved `runs/` beside the code it ran from, so a gitignored wrapper supplied the root. The privacy log has no `versions` line, so the commit is the tag's. ## E9. ELK hierarchy and the logic-only ablation

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | Does classifying the pinned MONDO and HPO OWL releases with ELK add subsumptions among the mapped concepts' ancestors to the hierarchy `bko.ontology` loads, and on E6's holdouts what do `bko-direct`'s data shares add over a draw from the ontology's logic alone? |
| Configuration | Design: ADR [`research/logic-only-ablation`](adr/research/logic-only-ablation.md) fixes the logic-only draw (each connected component of mapped concepts draws one assignment uniformly from those its `is_a` and nested constraints allow, closure read into fields, label-only concepts following the label), the named runs `e9-thin` and `e9-empty` (E6's frame, removal, holdouts and fill, arms `none`, `real`, `bko-direct` and `bko-logic`, no replica, candidate `bko-logic`, comparators `none` and `bko-direct`, criteria none, models `lr,hgb`), their slices `e9-thina` to `e9-thinj` and `e9-emptya` to `e9-emptyj`, and its Decision 8: a differing ancestor pair whose two classes are mapped concepts of a frame adds runs on the classified hierarchy. ELK comparison: `python -m bko.ontology elk` (ADR [`research/ontology-mapping-design`](adr/research/ontology-mapping-design.md), ELK comparison; [DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#elk-comparison)), ROBOT 1.9.11 (jar sha256 `53cd275c8e4c0dad1fc2c1c34174876795cd7d6b6926e1444adcbb0cb3c7ab26`), OpenJDK 26.0.1, 16 GB heap, over the pinned OWL releases (HPO `hp.owl` sha256 `8da35ec3b4cb494369b249acb9c2b3874a5a5839a91d5fae74b0d215b355138b`, MONDO `mondo.owl` sha256 `cbf3e02cc0aefed4fa6925162a5b1d1a48fbf0418586341967d5fd68fd7185d1`) against the OBO hierarchy `bko.ontology` loads (HPO `hp/releases/2026-09-01`, snapshot `93dace952fcb3ec4`; MONDO `releases/2026-10-06`, snapshot `2631e8e165d41def`), restricted to the mapped concepts and their ancestors on either side; each identity from `runs/e9-24/hierarchy.log`, which repeats `runs/e9-1/hierarchy.log`'s. Identity of the logic runs with E6 (`bko-direct`, `none`, `real` and the split must not move): `diff` of the sorted `arm holdout=<h> arm=bko-direct ...`, `arm=none` and `arm=real` lines, and of the `split seed=` lines, of `runs/e6-<k>/scores.log` against `runs/e9-<k>/scores.log` over `k` 25 to 34 and 35 to 44 (2 to 11 and 12 to 21 for the superseded runs). |

The DCR differences are plain paired t intervals over the 100 holdouts' `dcr-p5` and `dcr-median` added rows in `scores.csv`, which no compare line contrasts between the two arms. `none`, `real` and `bko-direct` read E6's means to every digit, as the identity diffs require. <br>**E2.** No logic-only E2 run: per-subgroup and population-level grounding read no prior, and `tests/test_gaps_logic_only.py` (`test_logic_priors_leave_subgroup_and_population_gaps_unchanged`, every dataset prior at 0.5 on the Synthea fixture) shows their gap listings unchanged; the frequency-aware score has no logic-only analogue (ADR Decision 7). The rerun reads the superseded `e9-1`, `e9-22` and `e9-23` figures to every digit: no arm here calls the API. |
| Read against | [RESEARCH.md section 4](RESEARCH.md#4-success-criteria); no verdict: `bko-logic`'s contrasts are unjudged (criteria none), and the ELK comparison has no criterion; [RESEARCH.md section 10](RESEARCH.md#10-related-work) places ELK beside a BKO |

## E10. Identifier perturbation

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | On E6's Diabetes 130 frame and removal, does rewriting `bko-direct`'s 19 unmapped fields through the Messages API (`bko-direct-perturbed`), or redrawing them by PRAM with no API call (`bko-direct-pram`), move its added rows away from the training records while keeping their column shapes and what they teach a classifier? |
| Configuration | Design: ADR [`research/identifier-perturbation`](adr/research/identifier-perturbation.md) fixes the 19 fields, the rewrite call, what it sends (Egress) and its refusals, PRAM at theta 0.5 on its own seed stream, the per-holdout distribution reading (column shapes, pair trends, column shapes per field and coherence against the label-1 training rows, logged on every holdout and refusing no row), the rewrite's per-call value quotas from the cell's label-group training rows, the pilots, the re-pilot and its gate G1-G6, and the full runs. The first pilots, `e10-3` and `e10-4`, ran under the delta check that preceded the reading: column shapes against the label-1 training rows, refused below the unperturbed rows' score minus `delta`, `delta` 0.036399 at f = 0.985 (run `e10-1`) and 0.003108 at f = 1.0 (run `e10-2`), each the `delta=` `perturb-threshold` logged over E6's stored `bko-direct` rows, and without quotas. The re-pilot, `e10-7` and `e10-8`, ran under the reading and the quotas. Pilots: `e10-pilot-thin` and `e10-pilot-empty` (E6's frame, removal and fill at f = 0.985 and 1.0, arms `none`, `real`, `bko-direct`, `bko-direct-perturbed` and `bko-direct-pram`, holdout seeds 0 and 1, candidate `bko-direct-perturbed`, comparators `bko-direct`, `bko-direct-pram` and `none`, ceiling 5.0 USD each), E6's generator settings (`claude-haiku-5-5`); the re-pilot reruns both at commit `2beac57593836cf0267c45a05f8cf917e8574b0d` (`e10-7`, `e10-8`), and again at tag `run/rerun-1` (`e10-38`, `e10-39`). Full runs: PRAM only, `e10-pram-thin` and `e10-pram-empty` (`E10_PRAM_THIN` and `E10_PRAM_EMPTY`: `E9_THIN` and `E9_EMPTY` with arms `none`, `real`, `bko-direct` and `bko-direct-pram`, candidate `bko-direct-pram`, comparators `bko-direct` and `none`, E6's 100 holdouts), each sliced as E6's into `e10-pram-thina` to `e10-pram-thinj` and `e10-pram-emptya` to `e10-pram-emptyj`; no arm calls the API, so the estimate is 0 USD and the ceiling kept from E6 is inert.
