## B3. CLLM arms

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | With the model, settings, holdouts and split fixed, what does asking the same model for records shown real training records, CLLM's way, give beside `bko`'s ontology guidance, and what does CLLM's curation add over the same prompt uncurated, on validity, drops, test and training copies, distance to closest record, replica fidelity and utility and E1's measures, over UCI and over the ablated Synthea frame? |
| Configuration | Arms: `cllm` and `cllm-uncurated` (`bko.evaluate.experiment.MESSAGES_ARMS`), each call guided by its cell alone and showing up to `bko.evaluate.cllm.SHOW` training records, the cell's own first; `cllm` drops each row its classifier curator confidently puts at the other label, `cllm-uncurated` keeps every one; both ask up to `CLLM_BUDGET` records per requested row, 22, the budget rule on B3's least keep share; `b3-1` to `b3-3` asked at a budget of 9 with shortfall-sized asks, so each of their rows holds at its run commit, where their call logs replay; the rerun's `b3-4` and `b3-5` asked at 22 (each `shortfall` line's `limit=` is 22 times its `quota=`) and hold at `run/rerun-1` (ADR [`research/cllm-arm-design`](adr/research/cllm-arm-design.md), which holds the prompt, the shown draw, the curator, the pilot that fixed the budget and each departure from CLLM). Runs: `b3-uci` and `b3-synthea` in `bko.evaluate.named_runs.RUNS`, each with arms `METHOD_ARMS`, `bko`, `cllm` and `cllm-uncurated` (`B3_ARMS`), replica arms the row-adding methods and those three, candidate `cllm`, comparators `METHOD_ARMS` and no criterion: `b3-uci` E1's holdouts, split and fill over UCI with a $20.00 ceiling; `b3-synthea` E3's removal, copy rule and Synthea frame, plus `real`, with a $35.00 ceiling. Settings: `SETTINGS` for all three Messages arms, which share one call log and ceiling; the in-run `bko` keeps `E1.budget`. The CLLM arms send training records whole ([DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#generation), What leaves the machine); both datasets' agreements are None. Estimate, fixed before the runs (`tests/test_evaluate_experiment.py::test_each_b3_ceiling_covers_its_estimate`): per arm, `e1-4`'s $10.51 over its three Messages arms on UCI and E3's $20.46 over its three on Synthea, times 1 for `bko`, f for `cllm` and 1 for `cllm-uncurated`, f each pilot's asks per requested row, the sum over its fills and cells of rows over keep share divided by its rows (1.1401 on UCI, 1.4432 on Synthea, from the Kept and Rows counts of ADR `research/cllm-arm-design`, The pilot); plus the shown records, `e1-4`'s 5490 calls over three arms (scaled on Synthea by $20.46 over $10.51) times (f + 1), times 20 records of an assumed 60 (UCI) or 100 (Synthea) input tokens each, at $0.10 per million input tokens: b3-uci $11.47 (1.25 times, $14.34, under its $20.00 ceiling) and b3-synthea $25.22 ($31.53 under $35.00). Run ids `b3-4` (`b3-uci`) and `b3-5` (`b3-synthea`), the rerun at `run/rerun-1`, superseding `b3-1` and `b3-3` (`b3-3`'s first launch, `b3-2`, was stopped; Runs), each rehearsed and then launched under the paid-run protocol ([DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation)). Readings, each `compare <run> <scores> --candidate <arm> [--beside <scores> --beside-arm <arm>]` into `runs/<id>-<word>/criteria.{csv,log}`: `-cllm` (`--candidate cllm`); `-raw` (`--candidate cllm-uncurated`); `-bko` (`--candidate bko --beside` the judged scores of its dataset, `runs/e1-5/scores.csv` or `runs/e3-7/scores.csv` (`runs/e1-4/scores.csv` or `runs/e3-4/scores.csv` for `b3-1` and `b3-3`), `--beside-arm bko`), which checks the in-run `bko` against them; `-inrun` (`--candidate cllm --beside` the run's own `scores.csv` `--beside-arm bko`), the ontology against in-context records; `-curation` (`--candidate cllm --beside` the run's own `scores.csv` `--beside-arm cllm-uncurated`), curation alone.<br>Measures, with no verdict, per dataset and Messages arm: validity (`passed` over `generated`, both fills) and drops by reason; for `cllm`, the rows reaching its curator and those it keeps, and its keep share per fill and flagged cell (read as [DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation) states it); test copies (the `copies` lines) and training copies (the `training` lines); the added rows' distance to closest record; replica fidelity (quality, column shapes, column pair trends) and replica utility; recall and AUC-PR at `all` and `flagged-subgroups` with the readings' intervals; on Synthea, recall on the removed cell's test cases against in-run `real`; and per-arm spend (each `validity` line's `usd=`).<br>Stops: a ceiling or projection stop ends the run for all three Messages arms and leaves that dataset's B3 unread, with no reading of a stopped run. |

The harness retried `tvae`'s fill on holdouts 4, 14, 26, 60, 76 and 81 (16 `retry holdout=` lines, `arm=tvae` and `arm=tvae-replica`), each recovered; one `py.warnings` line from joblib reads `UserWarning: A worker stopped while some jobs were given to the executor.` and the run went on. Each holdout logs `removal holdout=<n> cell=>=65/female/1 fraction=0.9 train=788 removed=47 kept=741` and `flagged holdout=<n> cells=>=65/female/1`, and each `test holdout=` line reads `records=197` and `kept=true`, with `copied` 160 to 181. Validity: `cllm` 137722 generated, 137658 passed, rate 0.9995 (gap 8422 and 8419, replica 129300 and 129239); drops 1 range (replica), 2 cell (gap), 4093 quota (234 gap, 3859 replica) and 57982 curation (6686 gap, 51296 replica); `bko` 76065 generated, 76062 passed, rate 1.0000; drops 0 range, 0 cell, 462 quota (replica); `cllm-uncurated` 75799 generated, 75768 passed, rate 0.9996; drops 0 range, 0 cell, 168 quota (replica). Curator: 137655 rows reached it (8417 gap, 129238 replica) and it kept 79673 (1731 and 77942), a keep share of 0.5788 (0.2057 gap, 0.6031 replica); the quota then dropped 4093 of those (234 and 3859); in the flagged cell `>=65/female/1`, gap 0.2057 (1731 of 8417) and replica 0.2108 (645 of 3060); its least replica shares are `<65/female/1`'s 0.0864 (2367 of 27400), then `>=65/female/1`'s 0.2108, `>=65/male/0`'s 0.2278 and `<65/male/1`'s 0.2670, against 0.9708 or more in the other four cells. Shortfalls: 5 `shortfall arm=cllm` lines over 4 holdouts (8, 14, 60 and 97), 20 rows short in all: `<65/female/1` 3 lines and 15 rows, `>=65/female/1` 2 and 5; so `cllm` added 1497 of the gap fill's 1500 rows and 74083 of the replica's 74100 (the `training` lines), and 75580 + 20 = 75600, the quota. On the augmented model, the ontology against in-context records and curation alone each contain zero at every scope; the intervals that exclude zero are replica ones: `cllm`'s replica is less faithful than `bko`'s on quality, column shapes and pair trends and trains a model with lower recall at `all`, and curation raises its replica's recall and AUC-PR at `all` over `cllm-uncurated`'s. |
| Read against | [RESEARCH.md section 4](RESEARCH.md#4-success-criteria); verdict: reported, no criterion applies. The CLLM arms are outside E1's comparison set, so their measures and `beside` intervals carry no verdict. On Synthea `cllm` added fewer rows than the other arms, 1497 of the gap fill's 1500 and 74083 of the replica's 74100 (Result, Shortfalls), so its contrasts compare a smaller fill |

## G1. Record generation through the Messages API

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | Does `bko.generate` turn each asked record into a candidate or a placeholder through the Messages API at each guidance level, and what does a call spend? |
| Configuration | Code: `bko.generate.messages` holds the call settings (`SETTINGS`), the structured-output schema (`record_schema`) and the guidance ladder (`Level`); `bko.generate.prompt` the prompt; `bko.generate.decode` the reading of a reply into candidates and placeholders; `bko.generate.spend.PRICES` the per-token prices each call's spend is computed at; ADR [`research/record-generation-design`](adr/research/record-generation-design.md) the design and its reasons. `temperature`, `top_p` and `top_k` are not sent. Settings (`SETTINGS`): model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `medium`, `max_tokens` 16000, `per_call` 20; the level is each run's. Runs `g1-1` to `g1-13` used model `claude-opus-5-5` at their commits, as each Runs row's served model records; runs `g1-15` to `g1-20` ran at `SETTINGS` at tag `run/rerun-1`. | Result | From runs `g1-15` to `g1-20`, served `claude-haiku-5-5` at tag `run/rerun-1`: every asked record decoded at every level. Per run, call 1 asks the `<55`, female, target 1 cell for 18 records (guidance basis ABSENT, rendered from level `findings` up) and call 2 the `>=55`, female, target 1 cell for 2 (basis SHOWN), both stop `end_turn`, and no placeholder was written. At `coherent` (`g1-16`), call 1 decoded 19 records for the 18 asked, and the run wrote all of them, as `bko.generate.decode` keeps every decoded item: `candidates rows=21`. Per level (call: asked, decoded, undecoded, stop reason, input / output tokens, USD as each `generate call=` and `run status` line prints it):<br>`cell` (`g1-15`): `1` asked 18, decoded 18, undecoded 0, stop `end_turn`, 1586 in / 3178 out, $0.001748; `2` asked 2, decoded 2, undecoded 0, stop `end_turn`, 1586 in / 511 out, $0.000414; run $0.002162<br>`coherent` (`g1-16`): `1` asked 18, decoded 19, undecoded 0, stop `end_turn`, 1618 in / 2362 out, $0.001343; `2` asked 2, decoded 2, undecoded 0, stop `end_turn`, 1618 in / 570 out, $0.000447; run $0.001790<br>`findings` (`g1-17`): `1` asked 18, decoded 18, undecoded 0, stop `end_turn`, 1758 in / 3324 out, $0.001838; `2` asked 2, decoded 2, undecoded 0, stop `end_turn`, 2316 in / 531 out, $0.000497; run $0.002335<br>`ontology` (`g1-18`): `1` asked 18, decoded 18, undecoded 0, stop `end_turn`, 2102 in / 3227 out, $0.001824; `2` asked 2, decoded 2, undecoded 0, stop `end_turn`, 3611 in / 562 out, $0.000642; run $0.002466<br>`profile` (`g1-19`): `1` asked 18, decoded 18, undecoded 0, stop `end_turn`, 2565 in / 3819 out, $0.002166; `2` asked 2, decoded 2, undecoded 0, stop `end_turn`, 4074 in / 582 out, $0.000698; run $0.002864<br>`summary` (`g1-20`): `1` asked 18, decoded 18, undecoded 0, stop `end_turn`, 2693 in / 3798 out, $0.002168; `2` asked 2, decoded 2, undecoded 0, stop `end_turn`, 4202 in / 583 out, $0.000712; run $0.002880<br>Input tokens grow with the level, as each adds one prompt block: call 1 from 1586 (`cell`) to 2693 (`summary`), call 2 from 1586 to 4202. Output tokens do not grow with it: they include each reply's thinking tokens (its usage's `output_tokens_details.thinking_tokens`, 586 to 2136 for call 1), and call 1's range from 2362 (`coherent`) to 3819 (`profile`). Runs `g1-15` to `g1-20` spent $0.0145 (the `end` lines' `usd` sum to 0.0144964); G1 as a whole (`g1-1` to `g1-13` and `g1-15` to `g1-20`) spent $0.7192. From the `generate call=` and `run status` lines of `runs/g1-15/candidates.log` to `runs/g1-20/candidates.log` and the `request`, `reply` and `end` lines of each run's `calls.jsonl` |

## G2. Direct generation from the fused BKB

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | What do records drawn from the fused BKB alone, with no API call, give on validity, drops, replica fidelity and utility, test copies and each E1 measure beside E1's `bko` arm, and how many gaps do they close under C1's reading? |
| Configuration | Code and design: `bko.generate.direct` draws each mapped concept present at its share in the fused BKB, `(n_c p_c + w p_g) / (n_c + w)` from the cell's and its label group's dataset-fragment priors, `w` being `bko.gaps.shares.GROUP_WEIGHT`, closes the drawn set under `is_a` in the draw log without reading the closure into fields, and copies each field group's values from a training record of the cell, else of its label group, that shows exactly the drawn concepts; ADR [`research/bkb-direct-generation`](adr/research/bkb-direct-generation.md) states its decisions and their reasons. Generation: `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.generate direct --request completeness --seed 0 --log runs/<run-id>/draws.jsonl`, seed 0, writing a schema 2 candidates file (generator `direct`, the seed, null definition and guidance) whose call plan is `chunks(quota, SETTINGS.per_call)`; run twice, its stdout and draw log compared with `cmp`. Closure: `python -m bko.fusion` over that file, as [C1](#c1-gap-closure-after-fusion) reads it, beside C1's figures (`c1-4-closure`, C1's rerun at `run/rerun-1`; `c1-2`'s before it). Arm: `bko-direct` in the `g2` named run of `bko.evaluate.experiment.RUNS` (`G2`: E1's holdouts, baselines, budget and attempts, the direct arm in place of the BKO arms and as the candidate, filling E1's gap request and a replica, no criterion), each attempt seeded by its request's seed, scored by `score g2 --candidates runs/<run-id>/holdout-candidates.jsonl` and read by `compare g2` with `--beside` E1's scores and `--beside-arm bko`: `e1-5`'s, the rerun's E1, whose `bko` arm generates at `summary` (`g2-2` read `e1-3`'s, at `ontology`). Data: the UCI archive `bko.datasets.sources` names and the default split for closure, E1's holdout seeds for the arm; releases as `bko.datasets.sources` pins them; each identity copied from the run's stderr. How the readings [RESEARCH.md](RESEARCH.md#4-success-criteria) leaves open are fixed: no criterion reads this arm, so each measure is reported with no verdict |

## G3. Guidance level

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | Which guidance level of `bko.generate.messages.Level` should E1's `bko` arm generate at: does a level other than `ontology` bring its replica closer to the training rows on both column shapes and column pair trends? |
| Configuration | Runs: one named run per level, `g3-cell`, `g3-coherent`, `g3-findings`, `g3-ontology`, `g3-profile` and `g3-summary` in `bko.evaluate.experiment.RUNS`, `g3-ontology` being a replicate of E1's level at the time (`ontology`), the null control, added on 2026-10-27 while the other five ran and before any of them was read, each `E1` with arms every comparison method and `bko`, replica arms the row-adding methods (`ADDS_ROWS`) and `bko`, a spend ceiling of $10.00, no criterion, and `SETTINGS` with only `guidance` set to its level. Model, data and releases are as E1's Configuration; the settings differ from `e1-3`'s only in `guidance`, so the level is the only difference from `e1-3`'s `bko`, which ran at `ontology`. The rerun at `run/rerun-1` (`g3-7` to `g3-12`) reads beside `e1-5`, the rerun's E1, at one commit with it: each level's settings differ from `e1-5`'s only in `guidance`, `e1-5`'s `bko` generates at `summary` (`SETTINGS.guidance`), and every rerun prompt holds the training-split bounds ([G4](#g4-effort-at-summary)), which `g3-1` to `g3-6`'s did not. So in the rerun `g3-summary` replicates `e1-5`'s level and is the null control, and `g3-ontology`'s `bko` (`g3-12`) is the `ontology` replicate the rule names. The rerun's plan (`scratchpad/rerun/ids.md`, gitignored) read each level only beside `e1-5` and named `g3-12` the null control; the readings against `g3-12` were set on 2026-11-10, after the readings beside `e1-5` (2026-11-09) had been seen, because the rule names `ontology` as its reference and `e1-5`'s `bko` is at `summary`. The Result reads each level against both (Decision). Runs records why the code that renders requests is unchanged at `ontology`: for `g3-1` to `g3-5`, `g3-replay`, a free replay of `e1-3` at `188b63a`, whose `src` differs from `a155d09`'s only in a docstring (`cmp` 0 of its LR rows); for `g3-6` at `1dd6730`, `sampler-replay` (G2's Runs), the `src` diff from its commit, and the identity of `g3-6`'s requests with `e1-3`'s. Run ids and commands, one launch per level under the paid-run protocol ([DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation)): `g3-1` `g3-cell`, `g3-2` `g3-coherent`, `g3-3` `g3-findings`, `g3-4` `g3-profile`, `g3-5` `g3-summary`, `g3-6` `g3-ontology`, each started as `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.evaluate launch g3-<n> g3-<level>` and read with `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.evaluate compare g3-<level> runs/g3-<n>/scores.csv --beside runs/e1-3/scores.csv`. The rerun: `g3-7` `g3-cell`, `g3-8` `g3-coherent`, `g3-9` `g3-findings`, `g3-10` `g3-profile`, `g3-11` `g3-summary`, `g3-12` `g3-ontology`, each read with `compare g3-<level> runs/g3-<n>/scores.csv --beside runs/e1-5/scores.csv` (Runs holds the commands as run). A stopped run is resumed (`launch --resume`, a new run id of this entry) or reported, never relaunched fresh, and every folder is recorded in Runs. Series: each `g3-*` scores file carries the gradient-boosting rows (`-hgb`) and `e1-3`'s does not (`e1-5`'s does); the `beside` reading logs 12 series (`bko.evaluate.beside.PAIRED`), all logistic regression, each at family 1, and the rule reads 2 of them per level, replica column shapes and column pair trends, 10 across the five levels. Decision: A level beats `ontology` on a measure when its paired corrected interval against `e1-3`'s `bko` (`compare --beside`, `E1.confidence`, family 1 as E1 reads met) has its lower bound above 0; in the rerun, whose E1 `bko` is at `summary`, the same interval against `g3-12`'s `bko`, the rerun's `ontology` level, a reference set after the readings beside `e1-5` were seen (`compare g3-<level> runs/g3-<n>/scores.csv --beside runs/g3-12/scores.csv` into `runs/g3-<n>-vs-g3-12/`). Keep the levels that beat it on both replica column shapes and column pair trends; if none, E1 stays at `ontology`. Among them take the level with the largest mean difference per measure; when the measures pick different levels, take the one earlier in `bko.generate.messages.LEVELS`, the less-guided level. The record states which measure decided, the multiplicity (10 intervals), and the noise floor. A level with no complete run (a projection or ceiling stop) or no `beside` interval leaves the decision open, and the record names the missing level. Noise floor: `e1-3`'s `bko` beside its `bko-population`, from `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.evaluate compare e1 runs/e1-3/scores.csv --beside runs/e1-3/scores.csv --beside-arm bko-population` (100 holdouts paired, 0 excluded): column shapes mean 0.001276 [-0.006971, 0.009522], column pair trends mean -0.000320 [-0.013013, 0.012374]; in the rerun, `e1-5`'s `bko` beside its `bko-population`, `compare e1 runs/e1-5/scores.csv --beside runs/e1-5/scores.csv --beside-arm bko-population` into `runs/e1-5-noisefloor/` (99 holdouts paired, 1 excluded): column shapes mean 0.000136 [-0.006775, 0.007047], column pair trends mean 0.000621 [-0.013311, 0.014552]. It is a method contrast, two gap definitions within one run, not run-to-run noise at one level. The null control is `g3-ontology` beside `e1-3`'s `bko` (`compare g3-ontology runs/g3-6/scores.csv --beside runs/e1-3/scores.csv`), and in the rerun `g3-summary` beside `e1-5`'s `bko` (`compare g3-summary runs/g3-11/scores.csv --beside runs/e1-5/scores.csv`): the same level in a second session, so its intervals show run-to-run noise; the record reports it beside the floor, and it does not change the rule, which reads the five other levels. Resolution: a level's lower bound clears 0 only once its mean difference passes about the floor interval's half-width, about 0.008 on column shapes and 0.013 on column pair trends (0.007 and 0.014 in the rerun), so "E1 stays at `ontology`" cannot be told from an effect below that size. Limits: each interval is family 1; ten are read and the selection across levels is uncorrected, so under a null with independent intervals about 1 - 0.975^10 = 22% of such studies would see some single interval clear; requiring both measures per level lowers it, and the levels share one baseline, so the true rate is unknown. The rule reads replica fidelity only; augmented recall and AUC-PR are reported beside it and not read by the rule. Controls: `cell` and `coherent` are the no-ontology controls: the Result reads `bko` at `ontology` over each (the sign-flipped `--beside` of `g3-cell` and `g3-coherent`; in the rerun, of their `--beside` against `g3-12`). Exclusions: Holdouts where any arm copied a test record are excluded and counted. If fewer than two remain, the `--beside` reading is refused and writes no table; the record reports the refusal and the run's own measures, from `compare g3-<level> runs/g3-<n>/scores.csv` without `--beside`. Spend estimate: per level one arm's share of `e1-3`'s spend, $4.27 (input-adjusted: `profile` about $4.42, `summary` about $4.46, `findings`/`coherent`/`cell` $3.64-$4.11; output per record assumed level-independent, unverified); total expected $19.80-$21.21 for the five levels, plus $4.27 for `g3-6` (approved by the user in session on 2026-10-27), $24.07-$25.48 in all; worst case 6 x $10.00 ceilings = $60.00. The ceiling counts logged calls; calls the SDK retries over HTTP are outside it (E1's `retried` count), so $60.00 is the ceilings' count, not a billed cap. Duration estimate: `e1-3`'s live holdouts took about 12.7 min each with three BKO arms, so one arm's share is about 7 h per run (100 x 12.7 / 3 min); if the five concurrent runs slow each other to `e1-3`'s pace, about 21 h (100 x 12.7 min). Both are estimates, not measurements. |

Against `ontology`, no augmented or `dcr-p5` interval excludes 0, and one replica-utility interval does, `profile`'s replica recall all +0.106667 [0.023125, 0.190208].<br>Decision, read two ways. (a) The rule of Configuration, stated before `g3-1` to `g3-6` were read, each level against `ontology` (`g3-12`'s `bko`, the rerun's reference, set after the readings beside `e1-5` were seen): the lower bound clears 0 on column shapes at `cell`, `profile` and `summary` (`coherent` -0.002273, `findings` -0.006290), and on column pair trends at `profile` only (`cell` -0.011648, `coherent` -0.017169, `findings` -0.019515, `summary` -0.000595). `profile` alone beats `ontology` on both measures, so neither the largest-mean step nor the tie-break is reached: the literal rule picks `profile`, where `g3-1` to `g3-6` read `summary`. `summary` misses on column pair trends by 0.000595. (b) Beside `e1-5`'s `bko` at `summary`, the rerun's E1: no level's lower bound clears 0 on column shapes or column pair trends, and `profile` cannot be told from `summary`: `g3-10` reads column shapes -0.001173 [-0.009001, 0.006654], column pair trends +0.002204 [-0.013155, 0.017562], each interval holding 0 and inside the null control's width (below). Against `ontology` the two levels' means differ by 0.0004 on column shapes (`summary` higher) and 0.0025 on column pair trends (`profile` higher), below the floor's half-widths (about 0.0069 and 0.0139). The floor's intervals are lines 180 and 181 of `runs/e1-5-noisefloor/criteria.log`. Multiplicity: 10 intervals read under (a), each at family 1, the selection across levels uncorrected (Configuration, Limits). The rule's decision is `profile`. This rerun keeps `SETTINGS.guidance` at `summary`, a stated departure from the rule decided after these readings were seen: every run of record in the rerun is at `summary`, and beside `e1-5` the two levels cannot be told apart. Whether to adopt `profile` and rerun the downstream runs is an [IDEAS.md](IDEAS.md#downstream-runs-at-profile-guidance) entry. This entry does not change `SETTINGS`; `SETTINGS.guidance` is `summary` (`bko.generate.messages.SETTINGS`), and E1's judged run `e1-5` runs under it.<br>Controls. `bko` at `ontology` over `cell` (the sign-flipped interval of `g3-7` against `g3-12`): column shapes -0.012373 [-0.021180, -0.003566], column pair trends -0.005949 [-0.023547, 0.011648]; over `coherent` (of `g3-8`): -0.006209 [-0.014690, 0.002273] and +0.002066 [-0.013036, 0.017169]. `cell` reads higher fidelity than `ontology` on column shapes, its interval wholly above it; `coherent`'s interval holds 0, and on column pair trends both hold 0. Ontology guidance did not beat its own controls on fidelity. `findings`, the first level that sends findings, reads no different from `ontology` on either measure. On column shapes `cell`, `profile` and `summary` read above `ontology`; on column pair trends only `profile` does, and `profile` and `summary` are the levels that add the reference group's concept shares and numeric quartiles (ADR [`research/record-generation-design`](adr/research/record-generation-design.md)).<br>Null control. `g3-11`, `summary` in a second session, beside `e1-5`'s `bko` at `summary`: its means are -0.000840 and -0.000494, with half-widths 0.0067 on column shapes and 0.0154 on column pair trends, close to the method-contrast floor's 0.0069 and 0.0139. So the run-to-run interval at one level is about the floor's width. Against `ontology`, `profile`'s means are 3.9 and 1.2 times the null control's half-widths, `summary`'s 4.0 and 1.0. |
| Read against | [RESEARCH.md: Evaluation contract](RESEARCH.md#5-evaluation-contract) (fidelity reported with no pass threshold) and this entry's decision rule; verdict: reported, no criterion applies. The decision rule, not a criterion of [RESEARCH.md](RESEARCH.md#4-success-criteria), reads the result. Against `ontology` (`g3-12`), the reference it names, the rule picks `profile`, the only level whose intervals clear 0 on both replica column shapes and column pair trends. Beside `e1-5`'s `bko` at `summary`, no level beats it and `profile` cannot be told from `summary`. The rule's decision is `profile`; the rerun keeps `summary` as a stated departure from the rule, decided after these readings were seen, since the two cannot be told apart beside `e1-5` and every run of record in the rerun is at `summary` (Result, Decision) |

## G4. Effort at summary

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | Does effort `low` meet `E1.validity` and stay non-inferior to `medium`, within δ, on `bko`'s three replica fidelity measures at `summary`, with the training-split bounds? |
| Configuration | Runs: `g4-low` and `g4-medium` in `bko.evaluate.experiment.RUNS`, each `SETTINGS_SWEEP`, the experiment G3's runs share: `E1` with arms every comparison method and `bko`, replica arms the row-adding methods (`ADDS_ROWS`) and `bko`, a spend ceiling of $10.00 and no criterion. Settings: `Settings(model="claude-haiku-5-5", effort="<effort>", max_tokens=16000, per_call=20, guidance=Level.SUMMARY)`, holdouts 0-99, the training-split bounds in the prompt. Model, data and releases are as E1's Configuration. `E1.confidence` and the family size are not logged: the Result cites them from `E1` at the run commit, the commit of each `scores.log`'s `versions commit=` line.<br>Run ids and commands: `g4-1` is `low` and `g4-2` is `medium`, launched together under the paid-run protocol ([DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation)) from one detached worktree at the run commit, each as `env PYTHONPATH=<worktree>/src <repo>/.venv/bin/python -m bko.evaluate launch g4-<n> g4-<effort> --in-flight 8 --data-dir <repo>/data` (16 calls in flight in all, the rate probe before E3). Each launch is supervised (ADR [`research/concurrent-call-waves`](adr/research/concurrent-call-waves.md); [DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation)): every `supervise` line is in the first folder's `launch.log`, `runs/g4-<n>/launch.log`, the last reading `supervise done segments=%d last=%s outcome=%s exit=%d`, and a run's folder of record is its last segment, `g4-<n>` or `g4-<n>-resume-<k>`. A stopped run is resumed by the supervisor or reported, never relaunched fresh, since a fresh run id would get its own ceiling; every segment folder is recorded in Runs. Each run is read at the run commit, `<low>` and `<medium>` being the two folders of record: `test ! -e runs/<low>/criteria.csv && test ! -e runs/<low>/criteria.log && env PYTHONPATH=<worktree>/src <repo>/.venv/bin/python -m bko.evaluate compare g4-low runs/<low>/scores.csv --beside runs/<medium>/scores.csv > runs/<low>/criteria.csv 2> runs/<low>/criteria.log`, and `g4-medium` alone into its own folder, `test ! -e runs/<medium>/criteria.csv && test ! -e runs/<medium>/criteria.log && env PYTHONPATH=<worktree>/src <repo>/.venv/bin/python -m bko.evaluate compare g4-medium runs/<medium>/scores.csv > runs/<medium>/criteria.csv 2> runs/<medium>/criteria.log`. The decision reads `runs/<low>/criteria.log`. The rerun at `run/rerun-1`: `g4-3` is `low` and `g4-4` is `medium`, launched with G3's rerun runs and read with the same two `compare` commands (Runs holds the commands as run).<br>Rule, fixed before either run: `low` is chosen only if (i) and (ii) both hold. (i) `g4-low`'s `bko` validity, read as `passed`/`generated` from the `validity arm=bko` line (not its four-decimal `rate=`), is at least `E1.validity` (19/20). (ii) For each of replica `quality`, `column-shapes` and `column-pair-trends` at scope `all`, the line `beside arm=bko against=bko run=g4-low level=summary source=replica measure=<measure> scope=all` is present, reads `undefined=0`, and its `low=` exceeds -δ (`E1.confidence`, family 1). δ is half of G3's `summary` gain over `ontology` ([G3](#g3-guidance-level)): quality 0.0162, column shapes 0.0168, column pair trends 0.0157 (the means of `g3-5` beside `e1-3`, G3's reading before its rerun, 0.032479, 0.033547 and 0.031410); the rerun keeps these δ. Its basis: it is half of the point-estimate gain of the level G3 selected, and a selected level's gain is biased upward; and that gain was measured without the training-split bounds. The two conditions form an intersection-union test, so no multiplicity correction applies. Otherwise the effort is `medium`: a missing `beside` line (a skipped series), `undefined` above 0 on any of the three measures, a refused `beside` reading (fewer than 2 paired holdouts) or a run with no complete record after the supervisor's retries each leaves `medium`. The Result reports the realized number of paired holdouts J, the `paired=` count of the `beside scores_sha256=` line. Cost does not enter the decision. `SETTINGS.effort` takes the chosen effort, so `SETTINGS` holds only measured combinations, as G3's level reached it.<br>Power at equality, approximate, from a simulation no file stores. Inputs: the 100 per-holdout differences of `bko`'s replica quality, column shapes and column pair trends at scope `all`, `runs/g3-6/scores.csv` minus `runs/e1-3/scores.csv`, two runs at one level (`ontology`), so their difference is run-to-run noise; their sample covariance is the per-holdout contrast covariance. Each draw takes J holdouts of zero-mean normal contrasts with that covariance and reads them as `compare` does: the corrected (Nadeau-Bengio) interval, mean ± t·s·sqrt(1/J + r), t the 0.975 quantile of Student's t with J - 1 degrees of freedom and r = 5/18, the held-out ratio `compare` reads from the scores; the rule passes when all three lower bounds exceed -δ. The range runs between two cases: contrasts independent across holdouts, and contrasts correlated across holdouts as the corrected interval assumes, through one effect per draw shared by its holdouts with covariance r times the contrast covariance (correlation r/(1+r)). 20000 draws per case, numpy's `default_rng(1)` seeding one stream for J of 10, 20, 30 and 100 in turn, the independent case first at each J. With 100 holdouts the rule passes about 0.66 (correlated) to 0.97 (independent) of the time when `low` equals `medium`. Column pair trends is the binding measure: alone it passes about 0.66 to 0.97, while quality is the mean of the other two in these scores, so it rarely binds. So a choice of `medium` cannot be told from a loss smaller than δ. A rule reading all three mean contrasts at least 0, G4's question read literally, passes in about 34% of draws at equality; this rule is a non-inferiority reading of that question.<br>Also reported: replica utility (the six replica utility series in `bko.evaluate.beside.PAIRED`), range drops, tokens per record asked, seconds per call and spend. Seconds per call is measured from each live call's `request` and `reply` `at` stamps in the call log (the reply's `at` minus the request's, per call number); a resumed segment re-stamps the calls it replays, which read about 0 s, so they are left out. The two runs share the API concurrently, so the two efforts' latencies are not separable. A run's spend is its last segment's `end` line `usd`, cumulative across segments with carried spend included; `live_usd` is one segment's. Runs checks that the cache token counts are zero, from each reply's `usage.cache_creation_input_tokens` and `cache_read_input_tokens`, since the `end` line holds no cache totals while `usd` prices them. Exclusions: as G3's.<br>Spend estimate: `medium` about $4.33, from `g3-5`'s 404.573 input and 211.349 output tokens per record asked (11980221 and 6258470 over 29612 asked) at `bko.generate.spend.PRICES`. Its direction under the bounds is unmeasured: the bounds add prompt text, and fewer top-ups may offset it. `low` may cost more than `medium` if it drops more candidates. Total estimate about $8.66; ceilings 2 x $10.00 on priced replies. The ceiling bounds priced replies only: attempts the SDK retried that the server bills, and calls abandoned on an idle stop, are outside it. Duration: not estimated; Runs records the measured one. |

(i) `g4-low`'s validity, passed over generated, is 25852/25854, at least 19/20: it holds. (ii) The lower bound against -δ: quality -0.016417 against -0.0162 fails; column shapes -0.010745 against -0.0168 holds; column pair trends -0.025028 against -0.0157 fails. Non-inferiority is shown on column shapes and not shown on replica quality (short of -δ by about 0.0002) or column pair trends (short by about 0.0093), so condition (ii) fails on two of its three measures and the rule chooses `medium`, as `g4-1` and `g4-2` read. The half-widths (0.0087 on quality, 0.0063 on column shapes, 0.0141 on column pair trends) are each smaller than δ, so the failures come from point estimates that lean negative: quality -0.007716 and column pair trends -0.010961, about 0.48 and 0.70 of δ. Every replica contrast's mean is negative (quality, column shapes, column pair trends, recall all and flagged, AUC-PR all and flagged) and no interval excludes 0: the sweep does not show `low` worse than `medium`, and does not show it within δ on two of the three fidelity measures. Column pair trends carries the decision; quality is the mean of the other two in these scores. Half of the rerun's `summary` gains against `ontology` ([G3](#g3-guidance-level): quality 0.021158, column shapes 0.026684, column pair trends 0.015633) would set a smaller δ (0.0106, 0.0133, 0.0078), under which (ii) also fails on quality and column pair trends. The power in Configuration is at equality and does not describe this observed case. ## G5. BKB-drawn concepts, LLM-filled fields

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | Does prescribing each record's concepts from the BKB change `bko`'s fidelity, validity and utility? |
| Configuration | Run: `g5` in `bko.evaluate.experiment.RUNS`: `E1` with candidate `bko-skeleton`, arms every comparison method plus `bko` and `bko-skeleton`, replica arms the row-adding methods (`ADDS_ROWS`) plus `bko` and `bko-skeleton`, a spend ceiling of $25.00 and no criterion. Settings: `SETTINGS`, `Settings(model="claude-haiku-5-5", effort="medium", max_tokens=16000, per_call=20, guidance=Level.SUMMARY)`, effort `medium` being [G4](#g4-effort-at-summary)'s choice, holdouts 0-99, the training-split bounds in the prompt. Model, data and releases are as E1's Configuration. The arm is read at the configuration E1 is judged on, beside an in-run `bko` control at the same settings.<br>The arm: `bko-skeleton` is a Messages arm guided by per-subgroup gaps, as `bko` is, whose every request carries, per asked record, the concepts `bko.generate.direct.sample` drew present at the attempt's seed, label-only ones left out, one line per record after the level's components (ADR [`research/record-generation-design`](adr/research/record-generation-design.md), What every level sends). Within one call a record is matched to a line by concept set, not by position, and only a record kept in the call's own cell consumes one: a record outside that cell is dropped as `dropped-cell`, one matching no line left unconsumed as `dropped-concepts`, and each line not consumed returns to the end of its cell's queue for a top-up, never redrawn (ADR [`research/e1-evaluation-protocol`](adr/research/e1-evaluation-protocol.md)). An attempt's lines are drawn at its own seed, so where `bko-skeleton` refused an attempt its retry draws at a derived seed `g2-4`'s `bko-direct` may not have drawn at: the `arm holdout=` lines (`attempt=`, `seed=`) of both runs' `scores.log` say which holdouts' draws differ.<br>Measures, with no verdict, per Messages arm: validity (`passed` over `generated`, both fills) and drops by reason, with `dropped-concepts` and its rate over `passed` (`dropped_concepts` over `passed` on the `validity arm=bko-skeleton` line); the lines re-queued (the sum of the `guided` lines' `requeued=`); replica fidelity (quality, column shapes, column pair trends) and replica utility (recall and AUC-PR, all and flagged); test copies (the `copies` lines); and per-arm spend (the `validity` line's `usd=`).<br>Stops: a ceiling stop ends the run for both Messages arms, which share one call log and ceiling, and leaves G5 unread, with no partial reading. After holdout 0's `holdout` line, the operator reads `bko-skeleton`'s `dropped_concepts` over `passed` from its `guided` lines: records asked per row run near 1 over (1 minus that rate), so a rate near 2/3 brings an attempt near `E1`'s budget of 3 records asked per row, and the operator then stops the run (F7, `kill -TERM <child_pid>`, [DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation)) rather than spend toward a late refusal.<br>Run id `g5-2`, the rerun at tag `run/rerun-1` that supersedes `g5-1`, launched under the paid-run protocol ([DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation)) with `--in-flight 8`; its folder of record is `runs/g5-2/`. Readings, at the run commit, `<worktree>` the frozen worktree of the run commit and `<repo>` this repository:<br>(i) the in-run control, `test ! -e runs/g5-2/criteria.csv && test ! -e runs/g5-2/criteria.log && env -u ANTHROPIC_API_KEY PYTHONPATH=<worktree>/src <repo>/.venv/bin/python -m bko.evaluate compare g5 runs/g5-2/scores.csv --beside runs/g5-2/scores.csv --beside-arm bko > runs/g5-2/criteria.csv 2> runs/g5-2/criteria.log </dev/null`;<br>(ii) the sampler alone, `mkdir -p runs/g5-2-direct && test ! -e runs/g5-2-direct/criteria.csv && test ! -e runs/g5-2-direct/criteria.log && env -u ANTHROPIC_API_KEY PYTHONPATH=<worktree>/src <repo>/.venv/bin/python -m bko.evaluate compare g5 runs/g5-2/scores.csv --beside runs/g2-4/scores.csv --beside-arm bko-direct > runs/g5-2-direct/criteria.csv 2> runs/g5-2-direct/criteria.log </dev/null`, `g2-4` being [G2](#g2-direct-generation-from-the-fused-bkb)'s run of record;<br>(iii) the judged run's `bko`, at the same settings, `mkdir -p runs/g5-2-judged && test ! -e runs/g5-2-judged/criteria.csv && test ! -e runs/g5-2-judged/criteria.log && env -u ANTHROPIC_API_KEY PYTHONPATH=<worktree>/src <repo>/.venv/bin/python -m bko.evaluate compare g5 runs/g5-2/scores.csv --beside runs/e1-5/scores.csv --beside-arm bko > runs/g5-2-judged/criteria.csv 2> runs/g5-2-judged/criteria.log </dev/null`, `e1-5` being [E1](#e1-uci-heart-disease-augmentation)'s judged run |

G5 runs two Messages arms, `bko` and `bko-skeleton`: 2 x $3.503 = $7.006. (b) The skeleton arm's user turn is longer by its block of lines. Over the 1800 `bko-skeleton` calls of the free rehearsal `score g5 --rehearse` at this entry's commit, the block holds 2640.8 characters per call on average (184.24 per record asked, 14.333 records asked per call), measured from the call log's `request` lines; `e1-4`'s call log gives 23287576 input tokens over 44714267 characters of system prompt and user turn, 0.52081 tokens per character, an upper rate for the block since the input tokens also count the output format. So 2640.8 x 0.52081 = 1375 more input tokens per call, and over `e1-4`'s 1830 calls per arm (5490 / 3), 1830 x 1375 = 2.517 million tokens at $0.10 per million (`bko.generate.spend.PRICES`), $0.25. Floor: $7.006 + $0.25 = $7.26. (c) A rehearsal follows every line, so it measures no concept drop. Allowance: the skeleton arm asks twice its rows, as a concept-drop rate of 1/2 of `passed` gives: $3.503 + 2 x ($3.503 + $0.25) = $11.01. An attempt asks at most 3 times its rows (`E1.budget`), so a skeleton arm that returns spends at most 3 x $3.753 = $11.26, $14.76 with `bko`; top-up calls ask fewer records each, so their fixed prompt costs more per record than this per-record basis carries, and refused attempts' retries add to it. ## G6. Generator model

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | With everything but the generator model fixed at the judged configuration, what do Sonnet 5.5 and three open models run on this machine give on validity, drops, tokens and compute per record asked, replica fidelity and utility and each E1 measure, beside the judged Haiku 5.5 `bko` at the same holdouts? |
| Configuration | Runs: `g6-sonnet`, `g6-gemma`, `g6-nemotron` and `g6-olmo` in `bko.evaluate.named_runs.RUNS`, each `G6_BASE` (E1's holdouts, split and fill with arms `METHOD_ARMS` and `bko`, replica arms the row-adding methods and `bko`, no criterion) at `SETTINGS` with the model alone changed (`G6_MODELS`): `claude-sonnet-5-5` through the Messages API with a $90.00 ceiling, and `gemma-4-26b-a4b-q8_0`, `nemotron-3-nano-30b-a3b-q8_0` and `olmo-3-7b-instruct-q8_0` through `llama-server` on `127.0.0.1` with thinking off and a $1.00 ceiling (ADR [`research/local-generator-backend`](adr/research/local-generator-backend.md), which records each file's digest, the server build and the probes). Sonnet 5.5's prices: ADR [`research/record-generation-design`](adr/research/record-generation-design.md). Estimate for `g6-1`: `e1-4`'s 300.535 input and 211.162 output tokens per record asked over its 25829 records asked give Sonnet 5.5 $70.07; for `g6-7`, `g6-1`'s call log at the tag's `PRICES` gives $39.8065 (Runs); the local models bill nothing. Opus 5.5 is not run ([IDEAS.md](IDEAS.md#opus-55-as-a-generator)). Readings, two per run, each beside `e1-5`'s `bko` (beside `e1-4`'s for the superseded `g6-1` to `g6-4`): the plain reading, `compare <run> <scores> --candidate bko --beside runs/e1-5/scores.csv --beside-arm bko` into `runs/<run-id>-judged/`, which pairs only holdouts where no arm of either run copied a test record and refuses fewer than 2 such holdouts; and the shared reading (ADR [`research/shared-test-split-reading`](adr/research/shared-test-split-reading.md)), which rescores the run with `e1-5` and `e1-5` with the run on each holdout's test split less every record either copies, into `runs/<run-id>-shared/` and `runs/<run-id>-sharedbase/`, and reads the two by `compare` into `runs/<run-id>-sharedjudged/` ([DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation) holds the commands, and the rescore with no `--with`, the self-check, writes `runs/<run-id>-selfcheck/`). `e1-5`'s `experiment` line, written at the tag, names every key, so the rerun's 100-holdout pairs pass the rescore's `experiment`-line check (check 2) with no assumed value. On the superseded pairs beside `e1-4` that check reads `e1-4`'s line, written at commit `48098d0`, which names no `frame`, `seeds`, `removal`, `training_fraction`, `decontaminate` or `threshold`, at each key's assumed value (ADR `research/shared-test-split-reading`), and the other line holds that value; the split check (each holdout's `split` line) and the arm-line checks pin the rest. Those shared readings ran at `7cfe087`, whose check 2 compared only the keys both lines name; the checks score nothing, and each G6 pair, in both directions, and each folder alone passes every check before scoring under the assumed values. Compute per record asked: tokens and FLOPs per record asked for a local model (FLOPs = 2 x active parameters x (prompt - cached + generated tokens), the cached tokens being the prompt tokens the server's usage reports as cached; active parameters from each GGUF header: Gemma 3.823B, Nemotron 3.580B, Olmo 7.298B); tokens and USD for an API model. No reading uses wall time, which depends on the machine (user decision 2026-11-01). Of the local runs only `g6-2` has a rehearsal: it bills nothing, so it checks the wiring and folder roles of `g6-2` to `g6-4` at the run commit, not their stop rules, and `g6-3`, `g6-4` and `g6-8` to `g6-12` ran without one; `g6-7` has its own. Each local run folder's `server.log` is the `llama-server` serve log for that run, not a file the harness writes: for `g6-2` to `g6-6` a copy of the operator's, for `g6-8` to `g6-12` the log `scratchpad/rerun/wave4/g6-local.sh` wrote there as it started the server. `g6-3`'s and `g6-4`'s begin with a failed first start: the server was pointed at the model file under the run's worktree (`<worktree>/data/models/...`), logged `failed to open GGUF file` with `(No such file or directory)` and `exiting due to model loading error`, and the second start loads the same file from `<repo>/data/models/`. Thinking-cost runs: `g6-gemmathink` and `g6-nemotronthink` (`G6_THINKING`), each `G6_BASE` on holdouts 0 to 4 at `SETTINGS` with the model alone changed to a `-thinking` id, `gemma-4-26b-a4b-q8_0-thinking` and `nemotron-3-nano-30b-a3b-q8_0-thinking`, at a $1.00 ceiling and an idle limit of 1700 s, a liveness guard and not a measurement; each serves its base model's weights file with `--jinja --reasoning on --reasoning-format deepseek`, so the server parses the reasoning out of the reply, and the harness reads the reply's text alone (ADR [`research/local-generator-backend`](adr/research/local-generator-backend.md), Thinking variants). The thinking runs are `g6-5` and `g6-6`, superseded by `g6-11` and `g6-12`. Their thinking-off comparison is the thinking-off run's calls on the same holdouts (`g6-2` and `g6-3` for `g6-5` and `g6-6`; `g6-8` and `g6-9` for `g6-11` and `g6-12`), those requested before each `scores.log`'s `holdout=4 done=5/` stamp, so no thinking-off control run is made. `g6-5` and `g6-6` have no `compare` reading; `g6-11` and `g6-12` have the plain reading, which pairs the five holdouts both runs hold, and their shared reading refuses, since a five-holdout run's `experiment` line differs from `e1-5`'s (Runs). Their compute is the formula above, the generated tokens including the reasoning; for `g6-5` and `g6-6` the reasoning and answer tokens of each reply are counted apart after the run by the running server's `/tokenize` (`scratchpad/thinking-measures.py`), and the two sum to the server's `completion_tokens` within the template's separator tokens; for `g6-11` and `g6-12` each call's `reasoning` line (`bko.generate.reasoning`) holds its reasoning tokens, counted by the same `/tokenize` during the run, and the answer tokens are the reply's `completion_tokens` less them, the separator tokens included. A 100-holdout run's tokens and FLOPs are projected two ways: projection A, the per-record-asked figures over holdouts 0 to 4 times the records asked in the thinking-off 100-holdout run (`g6-8` 25888, `g6-9` 26951; for the superseded runs `g6-2` 25879, `g6-3` 26920); projection B, the five-holdout totals times 20, which keeps the records a thinking-on run asks again. |

## G7. BKB likelihood curation

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | Does a candidate's log-probability under the fused BKB's concept draw separate the `bko` arm's records that match the real cell from those that do not, so that keeping the highest-scoring records would be worth a paid arm, on UCI Heart Disease (phase 0, `g7-5`; `g7-1` and `g7-2` before it) and on Diabetes 130 (phase 1, `g7-6`; `g7-3` and `g7-4` before it; phase 2 runs the arm)? |
| Configuration | Score, pool, split and gate as ADR [`research/bkb-candidate-curation`](adr/research/bkb-candidate-curation.md) fixes them before the run: each record's log-probability under `bko.gaps.likelihood.Likelihood` at its cell's shares (`bko.gaps.shares.shares`); per holdout, the `bko` arm's replica records whose outcome is `kept` or `dropped-quota`; each cell split at its own median, ties in `sha256(f"{holdout}:{call}:{position}")` order; quality, column shapes and column pair trends (`bko.evaluate.fidelity`, SDMetrics 0.32.0) of the pooled tops and pooled bottoms against the holdout's training rows, paired top minus bottom over E1's 100 holdouts with the corrected interval at `E1.confidence`, family 3. The gate separates when all three intervals lie above 0; a failure reads as "the score does not separate fidelity at the median". Input: [E1](#e1-uci-heart-disease-augmentation)'s judged run's own `runs/e1-5/holdout-candidates.jsonl` (schema 4, sha256 `a5f7d9925bd66bac34fa8f34a90d58ef32e7c12793aea066b7d5131f58a8d4a9`), read as written; `g7-1` and `g7-2` read `e1-4`'s (schema 1, sha256 `ba51769290460849d842d80142c468b31d2595f6d0eccbf46d90db07ef70a1ba`). Command: `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.evaluate likelihood e1 --candidates runs/e1-5/holdout-candidates.jsonl` ([DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation)); no API call. Diabetes 130's gate (`g7-6`) reads `e6-thin`'s `bko` replica candidates, from `runs/e6-25/holdout-candidates.jsonl` to `runs/e6-34/holdout-candidates.jsonl` as repeated `--candidates` (`g7-3` and `g7-4` read `runs/e6-2/` to `runs/e6-11/`'s), with `--data-dir data`, over `e6-thin`'s 100 holdouts. Curation raises `bko`'s removed-cell recall by 0.030 at a small all-records AUC-PR cost; it stays below `bko-direct` (0.4891), ADASYN (0.4833) and CTGAN (0.4768) at the same fraction (E6). |

## C1. Gap closure after fusion

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | Do at least half of the per-subgroup gaps of the UCI Heart Disease training split close once the generated records that pass validation are fused back into the BKO, and how many close, stay open or appear under each other gap definition? |
| Configuration | Commands, both from one commit, as [DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#gap-closure) gives them: `python -m bko.generate per-subgroup --guidance summary --request completeness`, which spends, then `python -m bko.fusion` over its stdout, which does not; the acceptance's one command is the second, and the first records its input. Code and design: `bko.gaps.definitions.completeness_request`, `bko.generate.candidates`, `bko.fusion`; ADR [`research/gap-closure-design`](adr/research/gap-closure-design.md) states which records join and how closure is read, and ADR [`research/bko-formal-gap-definition`](adr/research/bko-formal-gap-definition.md) the request. Data: the UCI archive `bko.datasets.sources` names and the default split, seed 0, each identity copied from the run's stderr; releases as `bko.datasets.sources` pins them. Generator: `bko.generate.messages.SETTINGS` at each run's commit, as its Runs row's served model records, at the level its row states (`c1-0`'s candidates at `cell` and `ontology`, `c1-1` and `c1-2` at `ontology`, `c1-3` and `c1-4` at `summary`, `SETTINGS`' level), guided by the per-subgroup gaps, whose findings block names the field states that ground each gap (ADR [`research/record-generation-design`](adr/research/record-generation-design.md)). Request: each cell holding a per-subgroup gap in the default training split, asked for `UNDERREPRESENTED_BELOW` rows rather than its shortfall; it is not E1's request, so G1's candidates do not answer it. How the readings [RESEARCH.md](RESEARCH.md#4-success-criteria) leaves open are fixed: the detected gaps are the per-subgroup (cell, statement) pairs of the default training split; a pair closes when detection over the training and joined records no longer lists it; the criterion is met when twice the closed per-subgroup pairs is at least the pairs listed before, and not met otherwise, with no interval, since one judged run is read; population-level and frequency-aware closure are reported beside it and judge nothing, population-level listing no gap on this split before the join ([E2](#e2-synthea-gap-detection-ablation)), so its share is undefined; count-based closure is not read, since this request fills cells by gap, not by count. The judged run: the first paid run that exits 0 with its call log ending `complete`; a run that fails or is interrupted after a call reached the API keeps its row with the reason, and the paid command runs once more under a new run id; a run whose calls returned is judged as it stands, placeholders included; a later paid run supersedes the judged run only when a change to `bko.generate` changes what a call sends (the request parameters the call log records, or the quota), or when it reruns the judged run's command at tag `run/rerun-1`, and any other paid run adds a row and changes no verdict; a change to the reading reruns the free command over the same candidates file and adds a row. Limits: closure depends only on which mapped concepts the joined records show; per-subgroup and population-level pairs cannot become new when records join, so a new count of 0 under either checks the implementation; frequency-aware reference shares move with any same-label cell's records, so it opens and closes pairs in cells that received none; `c1-0` closed the same per-subgroup pairs with G1's `cell`-level candidates, whose prompt names no gap, as with its `ontology`-level ones, so the result says the pipeline closes the gaps it detects, not that ontology guidance closes them, nor that a closed gap was filled with clinically right records; one run, no seed; the candidates file is gitignored, so its sha256 in Runs is the record. These readings were fixed after `c1-0`; the definition, split and request were fixed by ADR `research/bko-formal-gap-definition` before it |

## D1. Graded grounding and the frequency-aware level

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | How do the per-subgroup and population-level definitions read E2's ablations when a statement needs at least `k` records to be grounded, how does the frequency-aware definition read them at other posterior levels, and which level does the rule stated here choose? |
| Configuration | Commands: `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.gaps synthea` and `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.gaps uci`, the same commands and inputs as [E2](#e2-synthea-gap-detection-ablation), whose Configuration holds the population, releases, code mappings, scope, ablations, truth and cells; the sweep logs to stderr only, so each stdout matches E2's at the same inputs. Code: `bko.gaps.sweep` holds the settings and the rule; `bko.gaps.definitions.gaps` takes the grounding count as `grounding` and the level as `credibility`, defaults 1 and `CREDIBILITY`, the rules E2 ran (ADRs [`research/bko-formal-gap-definition`](adr/research/bko-formal-gap-definition.md) and [`research/frequency-aware-gap-score`](adr/research/frequency-aware-gap-score.md)). Settings: per-subgroup and population-level at each grounding count of `GROUNDING_COUNTS` (1, 2, 3 and 5) at the default level, a concept grounding statements in a fragment only when at least `k` of its records show it and a concept a cell assumes held at every `k`; frequency-aware at each level of `CREDIBILITY_LEVELS` (0.90, 0.95 and 0.99), which ignores the grounding count. Reading: on Synthea, every setting over every ablation of `E2_ABLATIONS`, the whole removals and every fraction of both, as sensitivity and specificity against the ablation's truth, as E2 defines them (`bko.gaps.experiment.detection`), from the `sweep ablation=` lines of stderr; the truth does not depend on the setting; on UCI, every setting's gap and cell counts on the default training split, seed 0, from the `sweep dataset=` lines. Rule, fixed before the run: the one the Level bullet of ADR [`research/frequency-aware-gap-score`](adr/research/frequency-aware-gap-score.md) states, `n/a` cases included; the `chosen` line of `synthea`'s stderr records its outcome with the measures that decided it ([DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#gap-detection) reads its fields). For the grounding definitions every `k` is reported and none is chosen: E1's judged arm keeps `k` of 1. `bko-frequency` reads the chosen level only once [RESEARCH.md: Detect](RESEARCH.md#detect) says so. Each Runs row names its commit, which stderr does not log, and copies the population, release and archive identities from its stderr |

Read from the eligibility definition (eligible = truth - before), not from a logged figure: a higher count lists more pairs before a removal, so it can shrink the eligible truth that sensitivity divides by, and sensitivity need not rise with the count. Frequency-aware at levels 0.90, 0.95 and 0.99: `cell` sensitivity 0.0811 at each level, specificity 1.0000; `condition` and each `condition` fraction sensitivity 0.0000 and specificity 1.0000 at each level; `cell-25`, `cell-50` and `cell-75` sensitivity 0.0000 at each level, specificity 1.0000 at 0.90 and 0.95 and 0.9809, 0.9807 and 0.9614 at 0.99. Chosen level: `chosen level=0.90 ablation=cell-50 floor=0.0000 sensitivity=0.0000 specificity=1.0000 tied=0.90,0.95`. The count-based sensitivity on `cell-50` is 0.0000, so every level qualifies; 0.90 and 0.95 tie at specificity 1.0000 above 0.99's 0.9807, and the tie-break picks the lower level, 0.90. No frequency-aware level recovers any `cell-50` truth pair, so the choice rests on specificity alone |
